2
by Proposition
3.8
. In particular,
\lambda>2
when
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ib by Theorem
[
17
, Theorem 1]
, and (
3.21
) holds when
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia since
k_{1}=A\left(z(k_{0}-1)+1\right)+1
by Proposition
3.5
(3) and
k_{1}=(z,A+1)\cdot(Az-1,A+k_{0})\cdot\lambda
by Lemma
3.7
(1)–(2) and (4).
∎

Corollary 3.10
.

Assume that Hypothesis
2.6
holds. If
\mathcal{D}
is a symmetric
2
-design, then one of the
following holds:

(1)

\mathcal{D}
is one of the two
2
-
(16,6,2)
designs as in
[
42
, Section 1.2]
;

(2)

\mathcal{D}
is the
2
-
(45,12,3)
design as in
[
44
, Construction 4.2]
.

Proof.

Assume that
\mathcal{D}
is a flag-transitive point-imprimitive symmetric
2
-
(v,k,\lambda)
design with
\lambda
is a prime number. Assume that
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is a
2
-design. Then

(3.22)

k_{0}k_{1}=\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\lambda

since
k=r
,
k=k_{0}k_{1}
,
r=\frac{v-1}{k-1}\lambda
, and
\frac{v-1}{k-1}=\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}

by Theorem
2.4
(1).Then (
3.22
) becomes

(3.23)

k_{0}\left[A(zk_{0}-z+1)+1\right]=\left(zk_{0}+1\right)\lambda

since
k_{1}=A(zk_{0}-z+1)+1
and
\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}=zk_{0}+1
by
Proposition
3.5
(2)–(3), and hence
k_{0}\mid\lambda
. Actually,
k_{0}=\lambda
since
\lambda
is a prime number and
k_{0}\geqslant 2
. Then
k_{1}=\lambda(\lambda+1)
and
v_{0}=\lambda(\lambda^{2}+\lambda-1)
by Theorem
3.9
(2). Then
k=\lambda^{2}\left(\lambda+1\right)
and

r=\frac{v-1}{k-1}\lambda=\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\lambda=\frac{\lambda(\lambda^{2}+\lambda-1)-1}{\lambda-1}\lambda=\lambda\left(\lambda+1\right)^{2}\text{,}

contrary to
r=k
. Therefore,
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is a symmetric
1
-design with
k_{1}=v_{1}-1
. Then
\lambda=k_{0}
and
k>\lambda(\lambda-3)/2
by Lemma
3.3
since
r=k
, and hence the assertion follows from
[
41
, Theorem 1.1]
. This completes the proof.
∎

Corollary 3.11
.

Assume that Hypothesis
2.6
holds. If
\lambda\leqslant 3
, then one of the
following holds:

(1)

\mathcal{D}
is one of the two
2
-
(16,6,2)
designs as in
[
42
, Section 1.2]
;

(2)

\mathcal{D}
is the
2
-
(45,12,3)
design as in
[
44
, Construction 4.2]
.

Proof.

Assume that that Hypothesis
2.6
holds. Assume
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is a
2
-design. Therefore, the parameters
v_{0},k_{0},\lambda_{0},v_{1},k_{1}
and
\lambda_{1}
are as Table
2
by Theorem
3.9
(2). Then
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is not of type Ic or IIb since
\lambda\leqslant 3
, whereas
\lambda\equiv 1\pmod{4}
in the former case, and
\lambda>v_{0}>3
by Lemma
3.1
in the latter. Thus
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia–Ib or IIa, and hence
\lambda>k_{0}\geqslant 2
, forcing
\lambda=3
. Then
\mathcal{D}_{1}
cannot be of type Ib, otherwise
(v_{0},k_{0},\lambda_{0},v_{1},k_{1},\lambda_{1})=(33,3,3/\mu,17,12,11\cdot 11/\eta)
by Theorem
3.9
(2), and this contradicts
[
18
, Lemma 3.6]
. Furthermore,
\mathcal{D}_{1}
cannot be of type IIa since there are no
2
-
(4,3,4)
designs.

Finally, assume that
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia, then
k_{0}=2
and
A=1
. Then
v_{0}=v_{1}=2(z+1)
,
\lambda_{0}=1
,
k_{1}=z+2
and
\lambda_{1}=3\frac{(z+1)^{2}}{\eta}
. Thus
r_{1}=3(2z+1)\frac{(z+1)}{\eta}
. Then
k_{1}\mid r_{1}
since
k_{1}\mid v_{1}r_{1}
and
(k_{1},v_{1})=1
. So
z+2\mid 9
, and hence
z=1
or
7
since
z\geqslant 1
. If
z=1
then
v_{1}=4
,
k_{1}=3
, and hence
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is a symmetric
1
-design, which is contrary to our assumption. Therefore
z=7
, and hence
(v_{1},k_{1})=(16,9)
. So
(v,k,\lambda,v_{0},v_{1},k_{0})=(256,18,3,16,16,2)
, which cannot occur by
[
18
, Lemma 3.5]
. Therefore,
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is a symmetric
1
-design with
k_{1}=v_{1}-1
. Now,
2\leqslant k_{0}\leqslant\lambda\leqslant 3
by Lemma
3.3
. Therefore,
(k_{0},\lambda)=(2,2),(2,3),(3,3)
. Actually,
(k_{0},\lambda)=(2,3)
by
[
17
, Theorem 2]
(obtained with the aid of the computer). Therefore,
(k_{0},\lambda)=(2,2),(3,3)
and hence
\mathcal{D}
is symmetric and so the assertion follows from Corollary
3.11
.
∎

An alternative proof of Corollary
3.11
is provided in
[
18
, Theorem 1.1]
. Our proof, as well as the proof of
[
18
, Theorem 1.1]
, relies on
[
17
, Theorem 2]
and
[
18
, Lemmas 3.5 and 3.6]
to exclude three numerical cases.

Based on Theorem
3.9
and Corollaries
3.10
and
3.11
, from now on we make the following hypothesis.

Hypothesis 3.12
.

The Hypothesis
3.4
holds,
r>k
and
\lambda>3
.

Recall that
\lambda_{1}=\frac{v_{0}}{k_{0}}\cdot\frac{v_{0}}{k_{0}\eta_{0}}\cdot\frac{\lambda}{\eta_{1}}
and
r_{1}=\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\cdot\frac{v_{0}}{k_{0}\eta_{0}}\cdot\frac{\lambda}{\eta_{1}}
by Theorem
2.4
(2.ii), Lemma
2.7
(4) and (
2.4
). Hence,

(3.24)

\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}=\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\text{.}

As we will see, we provide two different proof strategies based on the discriminant
\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}\geqslant\lambda
or
\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}<\lambda
; the following proposition is in this direction.

Proposition 3.13
.

Assume that Hypothesis
3.12
holds. Then one of the
following holds:

(1)

\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}\geqslant\lambda
,
A^{2}\leqslant v_{1}-1
and
v_{1}\neq\left(2A-1\right)^{2}
;

(2)

\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}<\lambda
and
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia or IIb.

Proof.

Assume that
\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}\geqslant\lambda
. Then
\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\geqslant\lambda
by (
3.24
), and hence
\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\geqslant A
since
\lambda\geqslant(k_{0}-1)A+1
by Proposition
3.5
(4). Then

v_{1}-1=A\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\geqslant A^{2}

since
v_{1}=A\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}+1
by Proposition
3.5
(3), which proves the first part of (1).

If
v_{1}=\left(2A-1\right)^{2}
then
A\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}+1=(2A-1)^{2}
again by Proposition
3.5
(3), and hence

(3.25)

(k_{0}-1)A+1\leqslant\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}=4(A-1)

since
\lambda\geqslant(k_{0}-1)A+1
. Thus,
k_{0}=2,3
or
4
. Actually,
k_{0}=3
since
zk_{0}+1=4(A-1)
by Proposition
3.5
(2). Then
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia by Theorem
3.9
since
\lambda>3
by our assumption. Further
z=\frac{4A-5}{3}
with
A\equiv 2\pmod{3}
, and
hence (
3.21
) becomes

(3.26)

A\left(\frac{8A-7}{3}\right)+1=\left(\frac{4A-5}{3},A+1\right)\cdot\left(\frac{1}{3}\left(4A^{2}-5A-3\right),A+3\right)\lambda

Now
\left(\frac{4A-5}{3},A+1\right)
divides
9
, and
\left(\frac{1}{3}\left(4A^{2}-5A-3\right),A+3\right)
divides
16
since
A\equiv 2\pmod{3}
. Therefore,

(3.27)

8A^{2}-7A+3=3\cdot\frac{9}{\alpha}\cdot\frac{16}{\beta}\cdot\lambda\leqslant 1728(A-1)

for some positive integer divisors
\alpha
and
\beta
of
9
and
23
, respectively. Thus
A\leqslant 215
, and easy computations show that no cases fulfill (
3.27
) since
\lambda
is an odd prime with
\lambda\geqslant 2A+1
and
\lambda>3
. This completes the proof of (1).

Assume that
\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}<\lambda
. Then
\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}<\lambda
by (
3.24
), and hence
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is not of type Ib–IIa since
\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}
is equal to
(\lambda+1)^{2}
,
\frac{\lambda(\lambda+1)}{2}
or
\lambda
, respectively. Thus,
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia or IIb by Theorem
3.9
, which is (2).
∎

Remark 3.14
.

If
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type IIb, then
A^{2}\leqslant v_{1}-1
and
v_{1}\neq\left(2A-1\right)^{2}
. Indeed, as shown in the proof of Lemma
3.7
(4),
A=v_{0}-3
. Hence
A^{2}\leqslant(v_{0}-2)(v_{0}-3)=v_{1}-1
. Moreover, if
v_{1}=(2A-1)^{2}
then
v_{0}-2=2v_{0}-7
since
v_{1}=(v_{0}-2)^{2}
, and hence
v_{0}=5
, whereas
v_{0}
is even (see Theorem
3.9
). Therefore
v_{1}\neq(2A-1)^{2}
.

4.
Group-theoretic Reductions

In this section, we prove the following reduction theorem for the
2
-designs
\mathcal{D}_{0}
and
\mathcal{D}_{1}
. In particular, we show that
\mathcal{D}_{1}
can never be of type Ib or IIb.

Theorem 4.1
.

Assume that Hypothesis
3.12
holds. Then
\lambda\mid k_{1}
,
\lambda>k_{0}
and one of the
following holds:

(1)

\lambda>\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}
and the following hold:

(a)

\mathcal{D}_{0}
is
2
-
(v_{0},k_{0},\lambda)
design admitting
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
as a flag-transitive automorphism group;

(b)

\mathcal{D}_{1}
is
2
-
(v_{1},k_{1},\lambda_{1})
design of type Ia.

(2)

\lambda\leqslant\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}
and the following hold:

(a)

\mathcal{D}_{1}
is
2
-
(v_{1},k_{1},\lambda_{1})
design of type Ia or Ic admitting
G^{\Sigma}
as a flag-transitive point-primitive automorphism group of affine or almost simple type;

(b)

\left|G^{\Sigma}\right|<\left|G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}\right|^{2}
for any
\Delta\in\Sigma
;

(c)

\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}>v_{1}^{1/2}
.

As we will see in the next sections, the usefulness of Theorem
4.1
is the following: either (1) holds, hence
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
contains large prime order automorphisms (namely automorphisms of order
\lambda
), and the pair
(\mathcal{D}_{0},G_{\Delta}^{\Delta})
is determined by using
[
35
]
; or (2) holds and much of the group-theoretic arguments for flag-transitive linear spaces, developed by Liebeck in
[
37
]
and by Saxl in
[
48
]
, can be transferred to
(\mathcal{D}_{1},G^{\Sigma})
. These arguments severely restrict the possibilities for
(\mathcal{D}_{1},G^{\Sigma})
.

4.1.
Notation

Throughout the remainder of this section, for simplicity, we denote the point set and the block set of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
by
\mathcal{P}_{0}
and
\mathcal{B}_{0}
, respectively, and the points and the blocks of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
by small and capital Latin letters, respectively. The parameters of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
will be denoted as usual. Finally, the group
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
is denoted by
\Gamma
. Hence,
\mathcal{D}_{0}=(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{B}_{0})
is a
2
-
(v_{0},k_{0},\lambda_{0})
design and
r_{0}=\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\lambda_{0}
admitting
\Gamma
as a flag-transitive automorphism group.

If
x,y
are any two distinct points of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
, then
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x)

denotes the set of blocks of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
containing
x
, and
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
denotes the set of blocks of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
containing the points
x

and
y
. Clearly,
\left|\mathcal{B}_{0}(x)\right|=r_{0}=\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\lambda
and
\left|\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)\right|=\lambda
. Moreover, if
X
is any subgroup of
\Gamma
and
Y
is any
X
-invariant subset of points, or blocks, of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
, we denote by
rank(X,Y)
the rank of
X
on
Y
, that is the number of
X
-orbits on
Y
.

Lemma 4.2
.

Assume that Hypothesis
2.6
holds and
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a
2
-
(v_{0},k_{0},\lambda)
design. If
\lambda>k_{0}
, then the following hold:

(1)

if
B
is any block of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
, then
rank(\Gamma_{B},B)=rank(\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{0})
;

(2)

if
x,y
are any two distinct points of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
, then the group
\Gamma_{x,y}
acts primitively on
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
. Moreover, denoted by
K
the kernel of the action of
\Gamma_{x,y}
on
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
, one of the following holds:

(a)

Z_{\lambda}\trianglelefteq\Gamma_{x,y}/K\leqslant AGL_{1}(\lambda)
;

(b)

A_{\lambda}\trianglelefteq\Gamma_{x,y}/K\leqslant S_{\lambda}
;

(c)

PSL_{j}(w)\trianglelefteq\Gamma_{x,y}/K\leqslant P\Gamma L_{j}(w)
, with
j

prime and
\frac{w^{j}-1}{w-1}=\lambda
.

(d)

\Gamma_{x,y}/K\cong PSL_{2}(11)
and
\lambda=11
;

(e)

\Gamma_{x,y}/K
is one of the groups
M_{11}
or
M_{23}
and
\lambda

is
11
or
23
, respectively.

Proof.

The group
\Gamma_{x,y}
permutes the elements of
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
. Let
U
be any
Sylow
u
-subgroup of
\Gamma_{x,y}
. If
u\neq\lambda
, then there is
B_{U}

in
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
fixed by
U
since
\left|\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)\right|=\lambda
. Thus
\left|U\right|\mid\left|\Gamma_{x,y,B_{U}}\right|
, and hence
\left|U\right|\mid\left|\Gamma_{x,B_{U}}\right|
. Let
B
be any fixed block of
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x)
. Since
\Gamma_{x}
is transitive on the set of blocks incident with
x
,
it follows that there is
\psi\in\Gamma_{x}
such that
B_{U}^{\psi}=B
. Thus
\Gamma_{x,B_{U}}^{\psi}=\Gamma_{x,B}
, and hence
\left|U\right|\mid\left|\Gamma_{x,B}\right|
. Therefore,
\frac{\left|\Gamma_{x,y}\right|}{\left|\Gamma_{x,y}\right|_{\lambda}}\mid\left|\Gamma_{x,B}\right|
.

If
\Gamma_{x,y}
is intransitive on
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
, then any Sylow
\lambda
-subgroup of
\Gamma_{x,y}
fixes each element
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
since
\left|\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)\right|=\lambda
. Therefore
\left|\Gamma_{x,y}\right|_{\lambda}\mid\left|\Gamma_{x,B}\right|
, and hence
\left|\Gamma_{x,y}\right|\mid\left|\Gamma_{x,B}\right|
since
\frac{\left|\Gamma_{x,y}\right|}{\left|\Gamma_{x,y}\right|_{\lambda}}\mid\left|\Gamma_{x,B}\right|
. Then

(4.1)

\left|y^{\Gamma_{x}}\right|=\frac{\left|\Gamma_{x}\right|}{\left|\Gamma_{x,y}\right|}=\frac{\left|\Gamma_{x}\right|}{\left|\Gamma_{x,B}\right|}\cdot\frac{\left|\Gamma_{x,B}\right|}{\left|\Gamma_{x,y}\right|}=r_{0}\frac{\left|\Gamma_{x,B}\right|}{\left|\Gamma_{x,y}\right|}\mathit{,}

and hence
r\mid\left|y^{\Gamma_{x}}\right|
since
\frac{\left|\Gamma_{x,B}\right|}{\left|\Gamma_{x,y}\right|}
is an integer. Thus, for any pair of distinct points
x,y
of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
either
\Gamma_{x,y}
is intransitive on
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
and
r_{0}\mid\left|y^{\Gamma_{x}}\right|
by the previous argument, or
\Gamma_{x,y}
is transitive on
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
and
\frac{r_{0}}{\lambda}\mid\left|y^{\Gamma_{x}}\right|
by Lemma
2.5
(2).

Let
y_{1},...,y_{s}
be the representatives of the
\Gamma_{x}
-orbits on
\mathcal{P}_{0}
distinct from
\{x\}
. Let
d_{i}=\left|y_{i}^{G_{x}}\right|
with
i=1,...,s
, and
\Theta\subseteq\{1,...,s\}
be such that the group
\Gamma_{x,y_{i}}
acts intransitively on
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y_{i})
if and only if
i\in\Theta
. Suppose that
\Theta\neq\varnothing
. If
\theta
denotes the size of
\Theta
, without loss we may assume that
\Theta=\{y_{1},...,y_{\theta}\}
. Then
r_{0}\mid d_{i}
for
i=1,...,\theta
since
\Gamma_{x,y_{i}}
acts intransitively on
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y_{i})
for each
i\in\Theta
. On the other hand,
\frac{r_{0}}{\lambda}\mid d_{i}
for
i>\theta
. Hence,
d_{1}+\cdots+d_{\theta}=e\cdot r_{0}
and
d_{\theta+1}+\cdots+d_{s}=f\cdot\frac{r_{0}}{\lambda}
for some non-negative integer
e
and
f
. Actually,
e\geqslant 1
since
\Theta\neq\varnothing
by our assumption. Therefore,

v_{0}-1=\left(d_{1}+\cdots+d_{\theta}\right)+\left(d_{\theta+1}+\cdots+d_{s}\right)=e\cdot r_{0}+f\cdot\frac{r_{0}}{\lambda}=\frac{r_{0}}{\lambda}(\lambda e+f)\text{.}

Thus
v_{0}-1=\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}(\lambda e+f)
since
r_{0}=\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\lambda
, and hence
k_{0}-1=\lambda e+f
with
e\geqslant 1
and
f\geqslant 0
. So
\lambda+1\leqslant k_{0}
, whereas
\lambda>k_{0}
by our assumption. Thus
\Theta=\varnothing
, and hence
\Gamma_{x,y_{i}}
acts transitively on
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y_{i})
for each
i\in\{1,...,s\}
. Therefore,
\Gamma_{x,y}
acts transitively on
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
for each point
y
of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
distinct from
x
. Then (1) follows from
[
11
, Lemma 7]
. Moreover,
\Gamma_{x,y}
acts primitively on
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
by
[
19
, Corollary 3.5B]
. Moreover
\Gamma_{x,y}/K
, where
K
is the kernel of the action of
\Gamma_{x,y}
on
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
, is as (2) by
[
19
, p. 99]
.
∎

Corollary 4.3
.

Assume that Hypothesis
3.4
holds and
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a
2
-
(v_{0},k_{0},\lambda)
design. Then the conclusions of Lemma
4.2
hold.

Proof.

Assume that Hypothesis
3.4
holds and
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a
2
-
(v_{0},k_{0},\lambda)
design. Then
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is a
2
-design, and hence
\lambda>k_{0}
by Theorem
3.9
. The assertion now follows from Lemma
4.2
.
∎

Proposition 4.4
.

Assume that Hypothesis
3.12
holds. Then
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is not of type Ib.

Proof.

Assume that
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ib. Then
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a
2
-
(\lambda\left(\lambda^{2}+\lambda-1\right),\lambda,\lambda/\mu)
design by Theorem
3.9
.
Since
\lambda\left(\lambda^{2}+\lambda-1\right)
is not a power of an
integer, it follows from the O’Nan-Scott theorem (e.g. see
[
19
, Theorem 4.1A]
) that
\Gamma
is almost simple.

Assume that
\mu=\lambda
. Then
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a linear space, and hence one of the following hold
[
9
, Theorem]
since both
v_{0}
and
k_{0}
are odd:

(1)

\mathcal{D}_{0}\cong PG_{m-1}(s)
,
m\geqslant 3
, and
(v_{0},k_{0})=\left(\frac{s^{m}-1}{s-1},s+1\right)
with
s
even;

(2)

\mathcal{D}_{0}
is the Hermitian unital of order
s
, and
(v_{0},k_{0})=\left(s^{3}+1,s+1\right)
with
s
even.

Then
\lambda=s-1
in both cases, and hence
\frac{s^{m}-1}{s-1}=s^{3}-2s^{2}+1
or
s^{3}+1=s^{3}-2s^{2}+1
according as case (1) or (2) occurs, respectively. Each of these equations does not provide admissible solutions, and hence cases (1) and (2) are excluded.

Assume that
\mu=1
. Hence,
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a
2
-
(\lambda\left(\lambda^{2}+\lambda-1\right),\lambda,\lambda)
design. Let
N
be the action kernel of
\Gamma_{B}
on
B
, then either
Z_{\lambda}\trianglelefteq\Gamma_{B}/N\leqslant AGL_{1}(\lambda)
and
\Gamma_{B}
acts
3/2
-transitively on
B
, or
\Gamma_{B}
acts
2
-transitively on
B
by
[
19
, p.99]
. Then
\Gamma
acts either
3/2
-transitively or
2
-transitively on the point set of
\mathcal{D}
, respectively, by Lemma
4.2
(1). Actually,
\Gamma
acts point-
2
-transitively on
\mathcal{D}_{0}
by

[
6
, Theorem 1.2]
since
\Gamma
is almost simple and
v_{0}
is odd and distinct from
21
. Further,
Soc(\Gamma)\ncong A_{v_{0}}
by
[
64
, Theorem 1]
. Then one of the following
holds by
[
32
, (A)]
since
v_{0}=\lambda\left(\lambda^{2}+\lambda-1\right)
is an odd composite integer, and
v_{0}>15
since
\lambda>3
:

(1)

Soc(\Gamma)\cong PSL_{m}(s)
,
m\geqslant 2
,
(m,s)\neq(2,2),(2,3)
and
v_{0}=\frac{s^{m}-1}{s-1}
;

(2)

Soc(\Gamma)\cong PSU_{3}(s)
,
s=2^{h}
,
h\geqslant 2
,
and
v_{0}=s^{3}+1
;

(3)

Soc(\Gamma)\cong Sz(s)
,
s=2^{h}
,
h\geqslant 3
odd, and

v_{0}=s^{2}+1
.

Note that
v_{0}-1=\allowbreak\left(\lambda-1\right)\left(\lambda+1\right)^{2}
. Then
2^{hl}=\allowbreak\left(\lambda-1\right)\left(\lambda+1\right)^{2}
with
l=3
or
2
in cases (2) and (3),
respectively. Then
\lambda=2^{u}+1
for some
1\leqslant u<hl
, and hence
\lambda+1=2^{u}+2
. Then
(2^{u}+2)^{2}=2^{hl-u}
, and hence
u=1
and
hl-u=4
.
So
hl=5
, which is not the case since
h,l>1
. Thus,
Soc(\Gamma)\cong PSL_{m}(s)
with
s=c^{i}
,
c
prime,
i\geqslant 1
and

(4.2)

\frac{s^{m}-1}{s-1}=\lambda(\lambda^{2}+\lambda-1)\text{.}

Clearly, the actions on the point sets of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
and
PG_{m-1}(s)
are
equivalent. Now, let
x
and
y
be any two distinct points of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
. Then a quotient group of
G_{x,y}
is as in Lemma
4.2
(2). On the
other hand, by
[
34
, Proposition 4.1.17(II)]
, the quotient groups
of
G_{x,y}
compatible with the list given in Lemma
4.2
(2) are those containing either a
c
-group or
PSL_{m-2}(s)
as a normal subgroup, or the solvable ones
of order a divisor of
(m,s-1)\cdot i
. The comparison of the two
information on
G_{x,y}
leads to the following possible cases: either
\lambda\mid s
and
m=3
, or
(m,s,\lambda)=(4,4,5),(4,5,5)
, or
\lambda=\frac{s^{m-2}-1}{s-1}
with
m>3
, or
\lambda=11
or
23
, or
\lambda\mid(m,s-1)\cdot i
. It is immediate to
see that the previous fourth cases out of five are ruled oud since they do
not fulfill (
4.2
). Thus,
\lambda\mid(m,s-1)\cdot i
,
and hence either
\lambda\mid s-1
, or
\lambda\mid i
and
\lambda\leqslant s^{1/2}
. The former implies
\frac{s^{m}-1}{s-1}\leqslant s^{3}-2s^{2}+1
, and hence
m\leqslant 3
, contrary to
\lambda\mid(m,s-1)
and
\lambda>3
. Therefore
\lambda\mid i
and
\lambda\leqslant s^{1/2}
, and hence
\frac{s^{m}-1}{s-1}\leqslant s^{1/2}(s+s^{1/2}-1)
. So
m=2
, and hence (
4.2
) implies
c^{i}\leqslant i(i^{2}+i-1)
with
c\geqslant 5
and
\lambda\mid(c+1,i)
, a contradiction. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.5
.

Assume that Hypothesis
3.12
holds. The one of the following holds:

(1)

\mu=1
and
\lambda
divides the order of
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
;

(2)

\mu=\lambda
and either
\lambda\mid v
, or
\lambda\mid v_{1}-1
, or
\lambda
divides the order of
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
.

Proof.

The assertion (1) immediately follows when
\mu=1
. Indeed, in this case,
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a
2
-
(v_{0},k_{0},\lambda)
design, and
r_{0}=\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\lambda
divides the order of
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
since
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
acts flag-transitively on
\mathcal{D}_{0}
.

Assume that
\mu=\lambda
. Therefore,
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a (possibly trivial)
2
-
(v_{0},k_{0},1)
design. Let
L
be any Sylow
\lambda
-subgroup of
G
. If
\lambda\nmid v(v_{1}-1)
, then
L
preserves an element
\Delta
of
\Sigma
by Theorem
3.9
. Hence,
L\leqslant G_{\Delta}
. Suppose that
\lambda
does not divide the order of
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
. Then
L\leqslant G_{(\Delta)}
, and hence
\Delta\subseteq F
with
F=Fix(L)
. Moreover,
N_{G}(L)
acts transitively on
F
by
[
57
, Theorem I.3.7]
, and hence
\mathrm{F}=\bigcup_{\sigma\in N_{G}(L)}\Delta^{\sigma}
since
\Delta\subseteq F
. Thus,
\left|F\right|=v_{0}c
with
c=\left|N_{G}(L):N_{G_{\Delta}}(L)\right|
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be the incidence structure
(F,\mathcal{B}_{F})
, where
\mathcal{B}_{F}=\left\{B\cap F:B\in\mathcal{B},\left|B\cap F\right|\geqslant 2\right\}
. Now, let
y,z\in F
,
y\neq z
, and
\mathcal{B}(y,z)
the set of blocks of
\mathcal{D}
containing
y
and
z
. Assume that
L
fixes an element
\mathcal{B}(y,z)
, say
C
. Then
L\leqslant G_{y,C}
, and hence
r=\left|C^{G_{y}}\right|=\left|G_{y}:G_{y,C}\right|
has order coprime to
\lambda
since
L
is a Sylow
\lambda
-subgroup of
G
, but this contradicts
r=\frac{v-1}{k-1}\lambda
. Thus, for any
y,z\in F
,
y\neq z
, the group
L
acts
transitively on
\mathcal{B}(y,z)
, and hence
B_{1}\cap F=B_{2}\cap F
for
any
B_{1},B_{2}\in\mathcal{B}(y,z)
. Thus,
\mathcal{F}=(F,\mathcal{B}_{F})
is a
2
-
(cv_{0},K_{F},1)
design, where
K_{F}=\left\{\left|B\cap F\right|:B\cap F\in\mathcal{B}_{F}\right\}
.

Let
B
be any
block of
\mathcal{D}
such that
B\cap\Delta\neq\varnothing
with
\Delta\subseteq F
, and let
\alpha\in G_{(\Delta)}
. Then
B^{\alpha}\cap\Delta=B\cap\Delta\textbf{}
, and
hence there is
\gamma\in L
such that
B^{\alpha\gamma^{-1}}=B
by the above argument since
\left|B\cap\Delta\right|=k_{0}\geqslant 2
. Therefore,
\alpha\gamma^{-1}\in G_{(\Delta),B}
since
\alpha\in G_{(\Delta)}
and
L\leqslant G_{(\Delta)}
. Thus
\alpha\in G_{(\Delta),B}L
, and hence

(4.3)

G_{(\Delta)}=G_{(\Delta),B}L

since
G_{(\Delta),B}L\leqslant G_{(\Delta)}
.

Let
x
be any point point of
\mathcal{F}
and let
B_{1}\cap F,B_{2}\cap F\in\mathcal{B}_{F}
containing
x
. We may assume that
x\in\Delta
since

N_{G}(L)
acts point-transitively on
\mathcal{F}
. Then there is
\varphi\in G_{x}
such that
B_{1}^{\varphi}=B_{2}
since
G
acts flag-transitively on
\mathcal{D}
. Note that,

G_{x}=G_{(\Delta)}N_{G_{x}}(L)=\left(G_{(\Delta),B_{1}}L\right)N_{G_{x}}(L)=G_{(\Delta),B_{1}}N_{G_{x}}(L)

by the Frattini argument since
G_{(\Delta)}\unlhd G_{x}
, and by (
4.3
). Hence,
\varphi=\zeta\vartheta
with
\zeta\in G_{(\Delta),B_{1}}
and
\vartheta\in N_{G_{x}}(L)
. Thus,
B_{2}=B_{1}^{\varphi}=B_{1}^{\zeta\vartheta}=B_{1}^{\vartheta}
with
\vartheta\in N_{G_{x}}(L)
. Therefore
B_{1}^{\vartheta}\cap F=B_{2}\cap F
, and hence
\mathcal{F}
is a
2
-
(v_{0}c,k_{F},1)
design with
k_{F}=\left|B_{1}\cap F\right|
admitting
N_{G}(L)
as a flag-transitive automorphism group. Then
N_{G}(L)

acts point-primitively on
\mathcal{F}
by
[
26
, Proposition 3]
, and hence
c=1
and
\mathcal{D}_{0}=\mathcal{F}
. Indeed, if
\Delta^{N_{G}(L)}\neq\left\{\Delta\right\}
, that is
c>1
, then
\Delta^{N_{G}(L)}
is a
N_{G}(L)
-invariant
partition of
F
since
\Sigma
is a
G
-invariant partition of
\mathcal{D}
. So
\lambda\mid v-v_{0}
, and hence either
\lambda\mid v_{0}
or
\lambda\mid v_{1}-1
since
v=v_{0}v_{1}
, contrary to our assumption. This completes the proof
∎

Proposition 4.6
.

Assume that Hypothesis
3.12
holds. Then
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is not of type IIb.

Proof.

The
2
-design
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is not of type Ib by Proposition
4.4
. Hence,
\lambda>k_{0}
by Theorem
3.9
. Now, assume that
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of IIb. Hence,
\lambda>v_{0}
again by Theorem
3.9
. Let
L
be any Sylow
\lambda
-subgroup of
G
, then
L
fixes at least a point
x
of
\mathcal{D}
since
v=v_{0}v_{1}
,
v_{1}=(v_{0}-2)^{2}
and
\lambda>v_{0}
. Then
L
preserves the element
\Delta
of
\Sigma
containing
x
, and hence
L\leqslant G_{\Delta}
. Actually,
L\leqslant G_{(\Delta)}
since
\lambda
does not divide the order of
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
, being
\lambda>v_{0}
and
\lambda
prime. Thus
\lambda
does not divide the order
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
and hence either
\lambda\mid v
or
\lambda\mid v_{1}-1
by Lemma
4.5
(2). However, this is impossible since
\lambda>v_{0}
,
v=v_{0}v_{1}
,
v_{1}=(v_{0}-2)^{2}
, and
v_{1}-1=(v_{0}-2)^{2}-1=(v_{0}-1)(v_{0}-3)
. Thus
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is not of type IIb.
∎

Theorem 4.7
.

Assume that Hypothesis
3.12
holds. Then one of the
following holds:

(1)

\lambda>\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}
and the following hold:

(a)

\mathcal{D}_{0}
is
2
-
(v_{0},k_{0},\lambda)
design admitting
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
as a flag-transitive automorphism group;

(b)

\mathcal{D}_{1}
is
2
-
(v_{1},k_{1},\lambda_{1})
design of type Ia.

(2)

\lambda\leqslant\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}
and the following hold:

(a)

\mathcal{D}_{1}
is
2
-
(v_{1},k_{1},\lambda_{1})
design of type Ia, Ic or IIa;

(b)

G^{\Sigma}
is a flag-transitive point-primitive automorphism group of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
of affine or almost simple type;

Proof.

Assume that
\lambda>\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}
. Then
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia by Propositions
3.13
(2) and
4.6
. Suppose that
\mu=\lambda
. Then
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a (possibly trivial)
2
-
(v_{0},k_{0},1)
design. Now,
\lambda\nmid v(v_{1}-1)
by Theorem
3.9
since
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia. Then
\lambda
divides the order of
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
since by Lemma
4.5
(2). Let
\sigma
be any non-trivial
\lambda
-element of
G^{\Delta}_{\Delta}
. Then
\sigma
fixes a point
x
of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
since
\lambda\nmid v_{0}
. Then
\sigma
fixes each line of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
containing
x
since
\lambda>\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}=\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}=r_{0}
. Any such lines is fixed pointwise by
\sigma
since
\lambda>k_{0}
by Theorem
3.9
(2), and hence
\sigma
fixes
\mathcal{D}_{0}
pointwise, a contradiction. Thus
\mu=1
by Lemmas
2.7
(2) and
3.2
, and hence
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a (possibly trivial)
2
-
(v_{0},k_{0},\lambda)
design. This proves (1).

Assume that
\lambda\leqslant\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}
. Then
A^{2}\leqslant v_{1}-1
and
v_{1}\neq\left(2A-1\right)^{2}
by Proposition
3.13
(1). Then
G^{\Sigma}
acts point-primitively on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
by
[
65
, Lemma 2.7]
, and hence
G^{\Sigma}
is either of affine type or of almost simple type by
[
65
, Theorem 1.1]
. Moreover,
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia, Ic or IIa by Theorem
3.9
and Propositions
4.4
and
4.6
. This proves (2).
∎

Proposition 4.8
.

Assume that Hypothesis
3.12
holds. Then
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is not of type IIa.

Proof.

Assume that
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type IIa. Then the group
G^{\Sigma}
is of order divisible by
\lambda
since
k_{1}
is divisible by
\lambda
by Theorem
3.9
and
G^{\Sigma}
acts flag-transitively on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
. Further,
G^{\Sigma}
is a flag-transitive point-primitive automorphism group of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
of affine or almost simple type Theorem
4.7
.

Let
\zeta
be any non-trivial
\lambda
-element of
G^{\Sigma}
. Then
v_{1}=\left(\lambda-1\right)^{2}=m\lambda+f
with
1\leqslant m<p
denoting the number of
p
-cycles of
\zeta
and
f\equiv 1\pmod{\lambda}
the number of fixed points by
\zeta
on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
. Then either
A_{\left(\lambda-1\right)^{2}}\trianglelefteq G^{\Sigma}\leqslant S_{\left(\lambda-1\right)^{2}}
or
\left(G^{\Sigma},\lambda\right)
is classified in
[
35
, Theorem 1.1]
. Suppose that the former occurs and let
\Delta
be any fixed point of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
. Then
A_{\left(\lambda-1\right)^{2}-1}\trianglelefteq G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}\leqslant S_{\left(\lambda-1\right)^{2}-1}
, and hence
A_{\left(\lambda-1\right)^{2}-1}
partitions
the
r_{1}
blocks of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
containing
\Delta
into
a
orbits of equal
length since
G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}
acts transitively on the
r_{1}
blocks of
\mathcal{D}_{1}

containing
\Delta
. Further
a>1
since
r_{1}>2
, and
a\mid\lambda\frac{\lambda+1}{2}
and
a\geqslant\left(\lambda-1\right)^{2}-1
since the
smallest non-trivial transitive permutation degree
A_{\left(\lambda-1\right)^{2}-1}
is
\left(\lambda-1\right)^{2}-1
. Then
\lambda=5
,
and hence
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is a
2
-
(16,10,9)
design and
A_{16}\trianglelefteq G^{\Sigma}\leqslant S_{16}
. Then
b_{1}=\binom{16}{10}
since
A_{16}
is point-
14
-transitive on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
, whereas
b_{1}=\frac{v_{1}r_{1}}{k_{1}}=24
. Thus this case is ruled out, and hence
\left(G^{\Sigma},\lambda\right)
is classified in
[
35
, Theorem 1.1]
.

Suppose that
Soc(G^{\Sigma})
is an elementary abelian
u
-group for some
prime
u
. Then
u=2
and
\lambda=2^{h/2}+1
with

h
a power of
2
since
v_{1}=(\lambda-1)^{2}=u^{h}
with
h\geqslant 1
,
h
even, and
\lambda
is an odd prime. Further,
h>2
since
\lambda>3
. By
[
35
, Table 1]
and bearing in mind that
\lambda
is a Fermat prime and
f\equiv 1\pmod{\lambda}
, the group
G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}
contains a normal subgroup isomorphic
to one of the groups
SL_{h/j}(2^{j})
,
Sp_{h/j}(2^{j})
,
\Omega_{h/j}^{-}(2^{j})
, or
PSL_{2}(17)
and
\lambda=17
for
h=8
, or one of the groups
A_{7}
and
\lambda=5
for
h=4
.

Assume that
G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}
contains a normal subgroup
N

isomorphic to one of the groups
SL_{h/j}(2^{j})
,
Sp_{h/j}(2^{j})
,
\Omega_{h/j}^{-}(2^{j})
. Then

r_{1}=\lambda\frac{\lambda+1}{2}=\left(2^{h/2}+1\right)\left(2^{h/2-1}+1\right)\text{.}

Suppose that
h>8
, then each of the integers
\lambda=2^{h/2}+1
and
\alpha=2^{h/2-1}+1
admits a primitive prime divisor by Zsigmondy’s Theorem (e.g. see
[
34
, Theorem 5.2.14]
). Since
\left|Out(N)\right|=2(h/j,2^{j}-1)j
,
2j
,
2j
according as
N

isomorphic to
SL_{h/j}(2^{j})
,
Sp_{h/j}(2^{j})
,
\Omega_{h/j}^{-}(2^{j})
, respectively, then
\left|Out(N)\right|\mid 2h
in each case since
j\mid h
. Thus,
\lambda\alpha\mid\left|N\right|
since
\lambda\equiv 1\pmod{h}
and
\alpha\equiv 1\pmod{(h-2)}
by
[
34
, Proposition 5.2.15(ii)]
, forcing
j=1
by
[
34
, Proposition 5.2.15(i)]
. By a direct inspection, the same conclusion holds for
h=8
since
r_{1}=17\cdot 7
divides the order
G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}
.

Note that,
r_{1}
is divisible by the index of a maximal parabolic subgroup of
N
since
N\unlhd G^{\Sigma}
and by
[
50
, 1.6]
.
If
N\cong SL_{h}(2)
, then

2^{h}-1\leqslant{h\brack t}_{2}\leqslant\left(2^{h/2}+1\right)\left(2^{h/2-1}+1\right)

with
1\leqslant t\leqslant h/2
by
[
34
, Proposition 4.1.17(II)]
, contrary to
h\geqslant 8
.

If
N\cong Sp_{h}(2)
, then

(4.4)

\prod_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{2^{h-2i}-1}{2^{i+1}-1}\mid\left(2^{h/2}+1\right)\left(2^{h/2-1}+1\right)

with
1\leqslant t\leqslant h/2
by
[
34
, Proposition 4.1.19(II)]
. Then
\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}(h-3i-1)<h
by using
[
1
, Lemma 4.1]
, and hence

t(h+2)/4\leqslant t(h-1-3(h/2-1)/2)\leqslant t(h-1)-3t(t-1)/2<h\text{,}

which implies
1\leqslant t\leqslant 3
. However, (
4.4
) is not fulfilled for
t=1,2
or
3
since
h\geqslant 8
.

If
N\cong\Omega_{h}^{-}(2)
, then

{h/2-1\brack t}_{2}\prod_{i=0}^{t-1}\left(2^{h/2-2-i}+1\right)\mid\left(2^{h/2}+1\right)\left(2^{h/2-1}+1\right)

with
1\leqslant t\leqslant h/2-1
by
[
34
, Proposition 4.1.19(II)]
(see also
[
51
, Exercise 11.3]
). So
2^{h/2-2}+1
divides
\left(2^{h/2}+1\right)\left(2^{h/2-1}+1\right)
, and hence
2^{h/2-2}+1\mid 3
, contrary to
h\geqslant 8
.

Finally, assume that
h=4
. Then
\lambda=5
and
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is a
2
-
(16,10,9)
design. Let
T=Soc(G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma})
, then
\left|G_{B^{\Sigma}}^{\Sigma}\cap T\right|=2
since
k_{1}=10
, hence
G_{B^{\Sigma}}^{\Sigma}T/T

is isomorphic to a subgroup of
G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}
of index
r_{1}/(k_{1}/2)=3
since
r_{1}=15
, a contradiction. The previous argument
can still be used to rule out the case
G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}\cong A_{7}
and
h=4
.

Finally, assume that
PSL_{2}(17)\trianglelefteq G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}
,
\lambda=17
and
h=8
. Then
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is a
2
-
(256,136,81)
design, and
G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}\leqslant PGL_{2}(17)
by
[
58
]
. Since

v_{1}\equiv 1\pmod{3}
and
v_{1}\equiv 4\pmod{9}
, there is an
element
\Delta^{\prime}\in\Sigma
,
\Delta^{\prime}\neq\Delta
,
such that
\left|\left(\Delta^{\prime}\right)^{G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}}\right|
is not divisible by
9
, but this contradicts Lemma
2.5
(2) since
r_{1}/(r_{1},\lambda_{1})=9
. Thus, this case is excluded.

Suppose that
Soc(G^{\Sigma})
is a non-abelian simple group. Then one of
the following holds by
[
35
, Table 2]
since
v_{1}=(\lambda-1)^{2}
,
\lambda>3
and
f\equiv 1\pmod{\lambda}
:

(1)

Soc(G^{\Sigma})\cong A_{m}
,
m\geqslant 5
,
(\lambda-1)^{2}=m(m-1)/2

and
2+\lambda\leqslant m\leqslant\frac{1}{2}(3\lambda-1)
;

(2)

Soc(G^{\Sigma})\cong PSL_{m}(w)
,
m\geqslant 3
, and
(\lambda-1)^{2}=\frac{w^{m}-1}{w-1}
;

(3)

Soc(G^{\Sigma})\cong PSL_{2}(w)
,
w>2
even,
(\lambda-1)^{2}=\frac{w(w+1)}{2}
and
\lambda=w-1
;

(4)

Soc(G^{\Sigma})\cong PSL_{2}(w)
,
w>2
even,
(\lambda-1)^{2}=\frac{w(w-1)}{2}
and
\lambda=w+1
;

(5)

Soc(G^{\Sigma})\cong Sp_{2m}(2)
,
m\geqslant 2
,
(\lambda-1)^{2}=2^{m-1}(2^{m}+1)
and
\lambda=2^{m}-1
;

(6)

Soc(G^{\Sigma})\cong Sp_{2m}(q)
,
m\geqslant 2
,
q
even,
(\lambda-1)^{2}=\frac{1}{2}q^{m}(q^{m}-1)
and
\lambda=q^{m}+1
;

(7)

Soc(G^{\Sigma})\cong HS
,
v_{1}=100
and
\lambda=11
;

(8)

Soc(G^{\Sigma})\cong PSL_{3}(3)
,
v_{1}=144
and
\lambda=13
;

(9)

Soc(G^{\Sigma})\cong PSU_{3}(3)
,
v_{1}=36
and
\lambda=7
.

Assume that (1) occurs. If
m\leqslant 10
, then
m=9
and
\lambda=7
since
(\lambda-1)^{2}=m(m-1)/2
. Therefore,
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is a
2
-
(36,21,16)
design,
b_{1}=48
and
A_{9}\trianglelefteq G^{\Sigma}\leqslant S_{9}
. This is impossible since
48
is not a transitive permutation degree for
G^{\Sigma}
by
[
13
]
. Thus,
m>10
. Let
\Delta
be any fixed point of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
, then
A_{m-2}\trianglelefteq G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}\leqslant S_{m-2}\times Z_{2}
. Then
A_{m-2}
partitions
the
G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}
-orbit consisting of the
r_{1}
blocks of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
containing
\Delta
into
\theta
-orbits each of equal length with
\theta\mid 8
since
A_{m-2}\trianglelefteq G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}
. Now,
r_{1}=\lambda\frac{\lambda+1}{2}
and
\lambda=\sqrt{\frac{m(m-1)}{2}}+1
and imply

\frac{r_{1}}{\theta}=\left(\sqrt{\frac{m(m-1)}{2}}+1\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{m(m-1)}{2}}+1\right)<\left(\frac{m}{\sqrt{2}}+1\right)^{2}<\binom{m-2}{3}

for
m>10
. Therefore, either
\lambda\frac{\lambda+1}{2}=\left(m-2\right)\theta
, or
\lambda\frac{\lambda+1}{2}=\frac{\left(m-2\right)\left(m-3\right)}{2}\theta
, or
m
is even
\lambda\frac{\lambda+1}{2}=\frac{1}{2}\binom{m}{m/2}\theta
by
[
19
, Theorems 5.2A]
. In the first case,
\frac{m(m-1)}{4}<\lambda\frac{\lambda+1}{2}\leqslant 16\left(m-2\right)
implies
10<m\leqslant 62
, and actually
m=50
and
\lambda=36
since
\lambda=\sqrt{\frac{m(m-1)}{2}}+1
, a contradiction. In the second case,

\theta=1
otherwise
\left(m-2\right)\left(m-3\right)\leqslant m(m-1)/2
,
contrary to
m>10
. Then
\lambda^{2}+\lambda=\left(m-2\right)\left(m-3\right)
and
\lambda^{2}-2\lambda+1=m(m-1)/2
. Then
\lambda=\frac{1}{6}\left(m-2\right)\left(m-7\right)
, which substituted in
\lambda^{2}+\lambda=\left(m-2\right)\left(m-3\right)
, leads to no admissible solutions for

m>8
. Finally, in the remaining case,
2^{m/2-1}\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\binom{m}{m/2}\leqslant m(m-1)/2
implies
10<m\leqslant 15
but one of these values of
m
fulfills
(\lambda-1)^{2}=m(m-1)/2
. Thus, (1) cannot occur.

In case (2), one has
m\neq 3
by
[
47
, A7.1]
. So
\lambda-1=11
or
20
by
[
47
, A8.1]
, a
contradiction. It is easy easy to see that (4) and (6) cannot occur, whereas
b_{1}=48
and either
PSL_{2}(8)\trianglelefteq G^{\Sigma}\leqslant P\Gamma L_{2}(8)
or
G^{\Sigma}\cong Sp_{6}(2)
, or
PSU_{3}(3)\unlhd G^{\Sigma}\leqslant P\Gamma U_{3}(3)
in (3), (7) or (9) , respectively. However, these groups are excluded since they do not have transitive permutation representations of degree
48
by
[
13
]
. The same argument rules out (7) and (8) since
HS\unlhd G^{\Sigma}\leqslant HS:Z_{2}
and
PSL_{3}(3)\unlhd G^{\Sigma}\leqslant PGL_{3}(3)
do not have a transitive permutation representation of degree
b_{1}=120
or
168
, respectively, by
[
13
]
. This completes the proof.
∎

Theorem 4.9
.

Assume that Hypothesis
3.12
holds. Then the following hold:

(1)

\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia or Ic;

(2)

\lambda
divides
k_{1}
and
\lambda>v_{0}
;

(3)

\eta_{1}=1
,
\eta_{0}=\eta
and
\eta\mid\frac{v_{0}}{k_{0}}
;

(4)

k_{1}<r_{1}
, and if
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia and
\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}\geqslant\lambda
then
2k_{1}<r_{1}
.

Proof.

Assertion (1) follows from Theorem
3.9
and Propositions
4.4
,
4.6
and
4.8
. Hence
\lambda
divides
k_{1}
and
\lambda>k_{0}
, which is (2). Furthermore, (3) follows from Lemma
3.7
(4) since
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is not of type IIa or IIb.
Now,

(4.5)

\frac{r_{1}}{k_{1}}=\frac{(v_{1}-1)\lambda_{1}}{k_{1}(k_{1}-1)}=\frac{A\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}}{A\frac{v_{0}}{k_{0}}\left(A\frac{v_{0}}{k_{0}}+1\right)}\cdot\frac{v_{0}}{k_{0}}\cdot\frac{v_{0}\lambda}{k_{0}\eta}\geqslant\frac{\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}}{A\left(\frac{v_{0}}{k_{0}}+1\right)}\cdot\frac{v_{0}\lambda}{k_{0}\eta}\text{,}

and hence

(4.6)

\frac{r_{1}}{k_{1}}>\frac{(v_{0}-1)v_{0}}{(k_{0}-1)(v_{0}+k_{0})\eta}\geqslant\frac{(v_{0}-1)k_{0}}{(k_{0}-1)(v_{0}+k_{0})}\text{.}

by Proposition
3.5
(3)–(4). Note that,
v_{0}>k_{0}^{2}
by Theorem
3.9
since
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia or Ic by (1) and
\lambda>3
. Then
\frac{(v_{0}-1)k_{0}}{(k_{0}-1)(v_{0}+k_{0})}>1
, and hence
k_{1}<r_{1}
by (
4.6
). This proves the first part of (4).

Assume that
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia and and
\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}\geqslant\lambda
. Firstly, note that
\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}\neq\lambda
since
\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}\mid v_{1}-1
by Lemma
2.5
, whereas
\lambda\nmid v_{1}-1
by Theorem
3.9
, being
\mathcal{D}_{1}
of type Ia. Then
\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}>\lambda
since
\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}\geqslant\lambda
, and hence
\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}>\lambda
by (
3.24
). Therefore,
v_{0}\geqslant\lambda+2
.

If
\eta
is a proper divisor of
v_{0}/k_{0}
, then the first inequality in (
4.6
) and the above argument imply
\frac{r_{1}}{k_{1}}>2\frac{(v_{0}-1)k_{0}}{(k_{0}-1)(v_{0}+k_{0})}>2
, which is the assertion in this case. It remains to consider the case
\eta=v_{0}/k_{0}
. By (
4.5
),
r_{1}>2k_{1}
is equivalent to

(4.7)

\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\lambda>2\left(A\frac{v_{0}}{k_{0}}+1\right)\text{,}

which certainly holds when either
k_{0}\geqslant 3
, or
k_{0}=2
,
\lambda\geqslant A+1
and
v_{0}>\lambda+2
since
\lambda\geqslant(k_{0}-1)A+1
by Proposition
3.5
(5) and
k_{0}\mid v_{0}
. The remaining case
k_{0}=2
,
\lambda\geqslant A+1
and
v_{0}=\lambda+2
cannot occur. Indeed,
k_{0}\mid v_{0}
,
k_{0}=2
and
\lambda
a prime force
\lambda=2
, whereas
\lambda>3
by our assumption.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.1
.

Assertions (1.a) and (1.b) follow from Theorem
4.7
(1.a) and (1.b), respectively. Further, assertion (2.a) follows from Theorems
4.7
(2.a)–(2.b) and
4.9
(1).

Since
v_{1}=A\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}+1
and
k_{1}=A\frac{v_{0}}{k_{0}}+1
by Proposition
3.5
(3), it follows that
v_{1}<2k_{1}
. This, together with
v_{1}=\left|G^{\Sigma}:G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}\right|
and
k_{1}=\left|G_{B^{\Sigma}}^{\Sigma}:G_{\Delta,B^{\Sigma}}^{\Sigma}\right|
, implies

(4.8)

\left|G^{\Sigma}:G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}\right|<2\left|G_{B^{\Sigma}}^{\Sigma}:G_{\Delta,B^{\Sigma}}^{\Sigma}\right|\text{.}

Assume that
\left|G_{\Delta,B^{\Sigma}}^{\Sigma}\right|>1
. Then
\left|G^{\Sigma}:G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}\right|\leqslant\left|G_{B^{\Sigma}}^{\Sigma}\right|
. Further,
\left|G_{B^{\Sigma}}^{\Sigma}\right|<\left|G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}\right|
since
k_{1}<r_{1}
by Theorem
4.9
(4), and hence
\left|G^{\Sigma}\right|<\left|G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}\right|^{2}
in this case.

Assume that
\left|G_{\Delta,B^{\Sigma}}^{\Sigma}\right|=1
. If
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia, then

\left|G^{\Sigma}\right|<2\left|G_{B^{\Sigma}}^{\Sigma}\right|\cdot\left|G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}\right|=2k_{1}\cdot r_{1}<r_{1}^{2}=\left|G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}\right|^{2}

by (
4.8
) and Theorem
4.9
(4) since
\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}>\lambda
, hence
\left|G^{\Sigma}\right|<\left|G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}\right|^{2}
. If
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ic, then

r_{1}\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{2}(\lambda+1)>\frac{1}{2}(\lambda-1)(\lambda^{2}-2)=v_{1}

by Theorem
3.9
, and again
\left|G^{\Sigma}\right|<\left|G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}\right|^{2}
. Therefore (2.b) holds true.

It follows from Corollary
3.13
, Proposition
3.5
(4) and (
3.24
) that

\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}=\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}>\lambda\geqslant A+1\text{.}

Hence,

(4.9)

\left(\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}\right)^{2}=\left(\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\right)^{2}>\left(\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\right)(A+1)>A\left(\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\right)+1=v_{1}\text{,}

which implies (2c). This completes the proof.
∎

5.
Reduction to the case
\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}>\lambda

In this section, we prove that case (1) of Theorem
4.1
cannot occur. The existence of
\lambda
-elements in
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
with
\lambda
a large prime in comparison to
v_{0}
, allows us to use
[
35
, Theorem 1.1]
to completely determine the possibilities for
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
, and from this derive that either
\mathcal{D}_{0}\cong AG_{n}(q)
with all planes as blocks and
\lambda=\frac{q^{n-1}-1}{q-1}
, or
\mathcal{D}_{0}\cong PG_{n-1}(q)
with all planes as blocks and
\lambda=\frac{q^{n-2}-1}{q-1}
. However, the parameters of such
2
-designs do not match with those otained for
\mathcal{D}_{0}
in Theorem
3.9
. Therefore no cases occur, and hence only (2) of Theorem
4.1
is admissible. More precisely, we prove the following result:

Theorem 5.1
.

Assume that Hypothesis
3.12
holds. Then
\lambda\mid k_{1}
,

.

\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}\mid\left(e_{1},...,e_{z},v_{1}-1,\left|T_{\Delta}\right|\cdot\left|Out(T)\right|\right)\text{.}

We want to focus to case (1) of of Theorem

. Hence, we make the following hypothesis and we show that it leads to no cases.

Hypothesis 5.2
.

The Hypothesis

Throughout the remainder of this section, unless differently specified, we adopt the same notation for
\mathcal{D}_{0}
,
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
:
\mathcal{D}_{0}=(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{B}_{0})
is a
2
-
(v_{0},k_{0},\lambda)
design (here
\lambda_{0}=\lambda
) admitting
\Gamma
as a flag-transitive automorphism group, where
\Gamma
denotes
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
. Here,
k_{0}\geqslant 3
by Lemma
5.3
, and
r_{0}=\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\lambda
. Finally, we denote
Soc(G_{\Delta}^{\Delta})
simply by
S
, and the set of blocks of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
containing any two distinct points
x
and
y
by
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)

In sequel of this section, we will often use the following fact.

Remark 5.4
.

Since
k=k_{0}k_{1}\leqslant v\leqslant 2\lambda^{2}(\lambda-1)
by Hypothesis
2.6
, and
k_{1}=A\frac{v_{0}}{k_{0}}+1
by Proposition
3.5
(3), it follows that

(5.3)

v_{0}+k_{0}\leqslant Av_{0}+k_{0}\leqslant 2\lambda^{2}(\lambda-1)\text{.}

Recall also that,
\lambda
does not divide any of the integers
v_{0}(v_{0}-1)
,
v_{1}(v_{1}-1)
or
v(v-1)
since
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is of type Ia.

5.1.
The affine case

In this section, we settle the case where
\Gamma
is an affine group. Hence,
S
is an elementary abelian
s
-group for some prime
s
. The point set of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
can be
identified with
c
-dimensional
\mathbb{F}_{s}
-vector space
V
in a way that
S
acts as the translation group of
V
and
\Gamma_{x}\leqslant GL_{c}(s)
, where
x
denotes the zero vector of
V
. Further,
H\unlhd\Gamma
with either
H=S:SL_{c/h}(s^{h})
or
H=S:A_{7}
for
(c,h,s)=(4,1,2)
by Lemma
5.3
. The symbol
H
will have this fixed meaning throughout this section.

Lemma 5.5
.

Assume that Hypothesis
2.6
holds. If
\lambda>k_{0}
,
\lambda>3
,
\lambda\nmid v_{0}(v_{0}-1)
and
\Gamma
is an affine group, then the following hold:

(1)

the blocks of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
are subspaces of
AG_{c}(s)
of size greater
than
s
;

(2)

\lambda
divides the order of
H_{x}
and does not divide the order of
\Gamma_{x}/H_{x}
.

Proof.

Firstly, we note that
k_{0}=s^{u}
for some
u\mid c
since
k_{0}\mid v_{0}
and
k_{0}-1\mid v_{0}-1
. Now, let
B
be any block of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
incident with
x
. Then
B
is split
into
T_{B}
-orbits of equal length
s^{j}
,
j\geqslant 0
, permuted
transitively by
\Gamma_{B}
since
S_{B}\trianglelefteq\Gamma_{B}
. Thus
\left|\Gamma_{B}:\Gamma_{x,B}S_{B}\right|=s^{u-j}
, and hence
\Gamma_{B}/S_{B}
is
isomorphic to a subgroup
J
of
\Gamma_{x}
such that
\left|J\right|=s^{u-j}\left|\Gamma_{x,B}\right|
. Also,
\Gamma_{B}/S_{B}
contains an
isomorphic copy of
\Gamma_{x,B}
. Then
r_{0}=\left|\Gamma_{x}:J\right|s^{j-u}

implies
\left|\Gamma_{x}:J\right|s^{u-j}=\frac{s^{c}-1}{s^{u}-1}\lambda
. If
u-j>0
, then
\lambda=s
which is not the case since
\lambda\nmid v_{0}
. Thus
u=j
, and hence
B
is a
\mathbb{F}_{s}
-subspace
of
V
, which is (1).

Suppose the contrary that
\lambda
divides the order of
\Gamma_{x}/H_{x}
. Then
\lambda\mid\left(\frac{c}{h},s^{h}-1\right)\cdot h
. Actually,
\lambda\mid h
since
v_{0}=s^{c}
and
\lambda
does not divide
v_{0}-1
. It
follows from (
5.3
) that

(5.4)

s^{c}<2\lambda^{2}(\lambda-1)\leqslant 2\frac{c^{2}}{h^{2}}\left(\frac{c}{h}-1\right)

since
\lambda>3
. Then
h=1
and
\left(v_{0},\lambda\right)=\left(2^{5},5\right),(2^{7},7),(2^{10},5)
, or
(2^{11},11)
. Only
\left(v_{0},\lambda\right)=(2^{10},5)
with
k_{0}=4
is admissible since
3\leqslant k_{0}<v_{0}
and
\lambda>k_{0}
. Then
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is not a symmetric
1
-design by Lemma
3.3
. Hence,
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is a
2
-design by Theorem
2.4
. Then
A=1
since
\lambda\geqslant\left(k_{0}-1\right)A+1
by Proposition
3.5
(4), and hence
k_{1}=2^{8}A+1=257
, contrary to
\lambda\mid k_{1}
. Thus
\lambda
does not divide the order of
\Gamma_{0}/H_{0}
, and hence
\lambda
divides the order of
H_{x}
since
\lambda
divides the order of
\Gamma_{x}
by Lemma
4.5
(1). This proves (2).
∎

Proposition 5.6
.

Assume that Hypothesis
2.6
holds. If
\lambda>k_{0}
,
\lambda>3
,
\lambda\nmid v_{0}(v_{0}-1)
If
\Gamma
is an affine group, then the following hold:

(1)

\mathcal{D}_{0}\cong AG_{c/h}(s^{h})
,
c/h\geqslant 3
, with all affine planes as blocks and
\lambda=\frac{s^{c-h}-1}{s^{h}-1}
;

(2)

Either
SL_{c/h}(s^{h})\unlhd\Gamma_{x}
or
(c,h,s)=(4,1,2)
and
A_{7}\unlhd\Gamma_{x}
.

Proof.

The assertion is clearly true if
(c,h,s)=(4,1,2)
and
H_{x}\cong A_{7}
. Hence, assume that
H_{x}\cong SL_{c/h}(s^{h})
. Let
y
be any non-zero vector of
V
. Since
\Gamma_{x,y}H_{x}/H_{x}\cong\Gamma_{x,y}/H_{x,y}
, it follows from Lemma
5.5
(2) that
\lambda

does not divide the order of
\Gamma_{x,y}/H_{x,y}
and hence the order of
\Gamma_{x,y}/H_{x,y}K
, where
K
is the kernel of the action
\Gamma_{x,y}
on
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
. Now,
\Gamma_{x,y}/H_{x,y}K\cong\left(\Gamma_{x,y}/K\right)/\left(H_{x,y}K/K\right)
with
H_{x,y}K/K\cong H_{x,y}/(H_{x,y}\cap K)
.
Then
\lambda
divides the order of
H_{x,y}/(H_{x,y}\cap K)
since
\Gamma_{x,y}/K
acts transitively on
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
with
\left|\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)\right|=\lambda
by Lemma
4.2
(2) and
\lambda
does not
divide the order of
\Gamma_{x,y}/H_{x,y}K
. In particular,
H_{x,y}K/K
acts transitively on
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
. Then one of the following holds again by Lemma
4.2
(2):

(1)

Z_{\lambda}\trianglelefteq H_{x,y}/(H_{x,y}\cap K)\leqslant AGL_{1}(\lambda)
;

(2)

A_{\lambda}\trianglelefteq H_{x,y}/(H_{x,y}\cap K)\leqslant S_{\lambda}
;

(3)

PSL_{t}(w)\trianglelefteq H_{x,y}/(H_{x,y}\cap K)\leqslant P\Gamma L_{w}(t)
, with
w
prime and
\frac{w^{t}-1}{w-1}=\lambda
.

(4)

H_{x,y}/(H_{x,y}\cap K)\cong PSL_{2}(11)
and
\lambda=11
;

(5)

H_{x,y}/(H_{x,y}\cap K)
is one of the groups
M_{11}
or
M_{23}
and
\lambda
is
11
or
23
, respectively.

On the other hand,
H_{x,y}\cong[s^{c-h}]:SL_{c/h-1}(s^{h})
by
[
34
, Propositions 4.1.17(II)]
. Hence, (1) and (5) cannot occur. In particular,
c/h\geq 3
. Moreover, either (2) holds with
(c/h,s^{h},\lambda)=(3,4,5)
or
(4,2,5)
, or (3) holds with
w=s^{h}
,
t=c/h-1
and
\lambda=\frac{s^{c-h}-1}{s^{h}-1}
, or (4) holds and
(c/h,s^{h},\lambda)=(3,11,11)
. The cases
(c/h,s^{h},\lambda)=(4,2,5),(3,11,11)
are excluded since they contradicts
\lambda\nmid v_{0}(v_{0}-1)
. If
(c/h,s^{h},\lambda)=(3,4,5)
, then
k_{0}=4
since
3\leqslant k_{0}<\lambda
. Therefore
v_{0}=64
,
k_{0}=4
and hence
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is not a symmetric
1
-design with
k_{1}=v_{1}-1
by Lemma
3.3
. Hence,
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is a
2
-design. Therefore,
v_{1}\geqslant 22
by Proposition
3.5
(3), and hence
v\geqslant 64\cdot 21
, whereas
v\leqslant 200
by
[
17
, Theorem 1]
.

Finally, assume that
\lambda=\frac{s^{c-h}-1}{s^{h}-1}
. The group
H_{x,B}
with
B
is a block of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
through
x
contains a Sylow
s
-subgroup
W
of
H_{x}
since
r_{0}=\frac{s^{c}-1}{s^{u}-1}\lambda
is coprime to
s
. Thus,
H_{x,B}
lies in a maximal parabolic subgroup of
H_{x}
. Therefore, there is a positive integer
e
such that

(5.5)

e{c/h\brack j}_{s^{h}}=\frac{s^{c}-1}{s^{u}-1}\cdot\frac{s^{c-h}-1}{s^{h}-1}\text{.}

Then either
j=1
and
u\mid\left(c,c-h\right)
, or
j=2
and
u\mid 2h
. Hence, either
u\leqslant h
or
j=2
and
u=2h
.

Assume that
u\leqslant h
. Now, the Sylow
s
-subgroup
W
of
H_{x,B}
fixes a point of
B\setminus\{x\}
, say
y
, since
k_{0}=s^{u}
by Lemma
5.5
(1). Then
B
is contained in the
1
-dimensional
\mathbb{F}_{s^{h}}
-subspace
\left\langle y\right\rangle
of
V
fixed pointwise by
W
since
k_{0}\leqslant s^{h}
and each non-trivial
W
-orbit on
V\setminus Fix(W)
has length divisible by
s^{h}
. Then
B
is fixed by
H_{x,y}
since since
H_{x,y}
fixes
\left\langle y\right\rangle
, but this contradicts the fact that
H_{x,y}
acts transitively on the
\lambda
elements of
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
. Therefore
u>h
, and hence
j=2
,
u=2h
and
k_{0}=s^{2h}
. Hence, the group
H_{x,B}
contains a subgroup
R\cong SL_{c/h-2}(s^{h})
fixing a decomposition of
V=V_{1}\oplus V_{2}
with
\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{s^{h}}}V_{1}=2
and
\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{s^{h}}}V_{2}=c/h-2
. Moreover,
R
fixes
V_{1}
pointwise and acts transitively on
V_{2}\setminus\{x\}
. This forces
B=V_{1}
since
x,y\in B\cap V_{1}
and
k=s^{2h}
. Therefore,
\mathcal{D}_{0}\cong AG_{c/h}(s^{h})
,
c/h\geqslant 3
with
\lambda=\frac{s^{c-h}-1}{s^{h}-1}
prime and all affine planes as blocks. This completes the proof.
∎

Corollary 5.7
.

Assume that Hypothesis
3.12
holds. If
\Gamma
is an affine group and
SL_{c/h}(s^{h})\unlhd\Gamma_{x}
then
\mathcal{D}_{0}\cong AG_{c/h}(s^{h})
,
c/h\geqslant 3
even, with all affine planes as blocks and
\lambda=\frac{s^{c-h}-1}{s^{h}-1}
.

Proof.

The proofs of Lemma
5.5
and Theorem
5.6
are clearly independent from the assumption
\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}<\lambda
. Thus they still work under the Hypothesis
3.12
, and hence the assertion follows.
∎

5.2.
The projective case

In this section, we settle the case
PSL_{c}(s)\unlhd G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}\leqslant P\Gamma L_{c}(s)
,
c\geqslant 3
,
s=l^{h}
,
l
prime,
h\geqslant 1
, which is the remaining case of Lemma
5.3
to be analyzed.

5.3.
Elations

Let
\pi
a projective plane (not necessarily Desarguesian), a collineation
\tau
of
\pi
that fixes a line
\ell
pintwise and a preserves each line through a (unique) point
C
of
\ell
is called
(C,\ell)
-
elation
of
\pi
. The point
C
and the line
\ell
are called the
center
and the
axis
of
\tau
. If
J
is a group of collineations of
\pi
, the subgroup of
J
consisting of the
(C,\ell)
-elations of
\pi
contained in
J
is denoted by
J(C,\ell)
. More information on groups of elations a of projective planes can be found in
[
29
, Section IV.4]
.

Proposition 5.8
.

Assume that Hypothesis
5.2
holds. If
PSL_{c}(s)\unlhd G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}\leqslant P\Gamma L_{c}(s)
,
c\geqslant 4
, then
\mathcal{D}_{0}\cong PG_{c-1}(s)
with all planes as blocks and
\lambda=\frac{s^{c-2}-1}{s-1}
.

Proof.

Recall that
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a
2
-
\left(\frac{s^{c}-1}{s-1},k_{0},\lambda\right)
design with
\lambda\mid s^{i}-1
,
c>i
admitting
\Gamma
as a flag-transitive automorphism group by Lemma
5.3
. Moreover,
\lambda
fulfills (
3.21
) by Theorem
3.9
since
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia by Theorem
4.1
(1.b). The group
\Gamma
has two
2
-transitive permutation representations of degree
\frac{s^{c}-1}{s-1}
: one on the set of points of
PG_{c-1}(s)
, the other on the set of hyperplanes of
PG_{c-1}(s)
. The two conjugacy classes in
\Gamma
of the point-stabilizers and hyperplane-stabilizers are fused by a polarity of
PG_{c-1}(s)
. Thus, we may identify the point set of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
with that of
PG_{c-1}(s)
.

Let
x,y
be any two distinct points of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
and let
\ell
be the line of
PG_{c-1}(s)
containing them. Since
\left|Out(\Gamma)\right|=2(c,s-1)\cdot\log_{l}(s)
,
\lambda\nmid v_{0}(v_{0}-1)
,
\lambda>2
and
(c,s-1)=\left(\frac{s^{c}-1}{s-1},s-1\right)
, it follows from (
5.3
) that

(5.6)

\frac{s^{c}-1}{s-1}<2\cdot(\log_{l}s-1)\cdot\log_{l}^{2}s

However, (
5.6
) admits no solutions for
c\geqslant 3
.
Thus
\lambda
does not divide the order of
\mathrm{Out}(\Gamma)
, and hence it does not divide the order of
\Gamma_{x,y}/S_{x,y}
since
\left|\Gamma_{x,y}/S_{x,y}\right|\mid\left|\mathrm{Out}(\Gamma)\right|
. Consequently,
\lambda
does not divide the order of
\Gamma_{x,y}/S_{x,y}K
, where
K
is the kernel of the action
\Gamma_{x,y}
on
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
. Now,
\Gamma_{x,y}/S_{x,y}K\cong\left(\Gamma_{x,y}/K\right)/\left(S_{x,y}K/K\right)
with
S_{x,y}K/K\cong S_{x,y}/(H_{x,y}\cap K)
.
Then
\lambda
divides the order of
S_{x,y}/(S_{x,y}\cap K)
since
\Gamma_{x,y}/K
acts transitively on
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
with
\left|\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)\right|=\lambda
by Lemma
4.2
(2) and
\lambda
does not
divide the order of
\Gamma_{x,y}/S_{x,y}K
. Then one of the following holds:

(1)

Z_{\lambda}\trianglelefteq S_{x,y}/(S_{x,y}\cap K)\leqslant AGL_{1}(\lambda)
;

(2)

A_{\lambda}\trianglelefteq S_{x,y}/(S_{x,y}\cap K)\leqslant S_{\lambda}
;

(3)

PSL_{t}(w)\trianglelefteq S_{x,y}/(S_{x,y}\cap K)\leqslant P\Gamma L_{t}(w)
, with
t
prime and
\frac{w^{t}-1}{w-1}=\lambda
.

(4)

S_{x,y}/(S_{x,y}\cap K)\cong PSL_{2}(11)
and
\lambda=11
;

(5)

S_{x,y}/(S_{x,y}\cap K)
is one of the groups
M_{11}
or
M_{23}
and
\lambda
is
11
or
23
, respectively.

If (1) holds, then
\lambda\mid s-1
y
[
34
, Propositions 4.1.17(II)]
, whereas
\lambda\mid s^{i}-1
with
i>s/2
. Hence, (1) is ruleed out. Now, the socle of the almost simple quotient groups of
S_{x,y}
is
PSL_{c-2}(s)
again by
[
34
, Propositions 4.1.17(II)]
. Then either (2) holds with
c=3
,
s=4,5
and
\lambda=5
, or (3) holds with
w=s
,
t=c-2
and
\lambda=\frac{s^{c-2}-1}{s-1}
, or (5) holds with
s=\lambda=11
. Case (2) with
s=5
and case (4) are excluded since they contradict
\lambda\nmid v_{0}-1
. Further, (
5.3
) implies
4^{c}<601
in case (4) with
s=4
. Therefore
c=3
or
4
, but both cases are ruled out since they contradict
\lambda\nmid v_{0}(v_{0}-1)
.

Finally, assume that
\lambda=\frac{s^{c-2}-1}{s-1}
with
c-2
a prime number. Let
B\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
and set
\theta=\left|B\cap\ell\right|
, where
\ell
is the line of
PG_{c-1}(s)
containing both
x
and
y
. Let us count the pairs
\left(z,C\right)
with
C\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
and
z
a point of
PG_{c-1}(s)\setminus\ell
such that
z\in C\setminus\{x,y\}
. Since
\Gamma_{x,y}
acts transitively on
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
and on
PG_{c-1}(s)\setminus\ell
, it follows that
k_{0}-\theta
is the number of points lying in
PG_{c-1}(s)\setminus\ell
contained in any element of
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
, hence

(5.7)

\lambda(k_{0}-\theta)=s^{2}\frac{s^{c-2}-1}{s-1}\alpha\text{,}

where
\alpha
is the constant number of elements of
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
containing any fixed point of
PG_{c-1}(s)\setminus\ell
. Then
s^{2}\mid k_{0}-\theta
since
\lambda=\frac{s^{c-2}-1}{s-1}
.

If
\theta=2
then
s^{2}\mid k_{0}-2
; if
\theta>2
, let us count the pairs
\left(E,z^{\prime\prime}\right)
with
E\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
and
z^{\prime\prime}\in\ell\setminus\{x,y\}
such that
z^{\prime\prime}\in E
. It follows that
(s-1)\beta=\lambda(\theta-2)
, where
\beta
is the constant number of elements of
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
containing a fixed point of
\ell\setminus\{x,y\}
, since
\Gamma_{x,y}
acts transitively on
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x,y)
and on
\ell\setminus\{x,y\}
. Clearly,
\beta\leqslant\lambda
. Since
\lambda=\frac{s^{c-2}-1}{s-1}
with
c-2
a prime number, it follows that
\left(\lambda,s-1\right)=1
. Thus
\beta=\lambda
, and hence
\theta=s+1
. Thus, we obtain
s^{2}\mid k_{0}-s-1
. Therefore, we have proven that either
\theta=2
and
s^{2}\mid k_{0}-2
, or
\theta=s+1
and
s^{2}\mid k_{0}-s-1
.

If
\ell^{\prime}
is any other line of
PG_{c-1}(s)
such that
B\cap\ell^{\prime}=\{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}\}
, we may repeat the above argument with
x^{\prime},y^{\prime}
,
\ell^{\prime}
and
\theta^{\prime}
in the role of
x,y
,
\ell
and
\theta
, thus obtaining
\theta=2
and
s^{2}\mid k_{0}-2
, or
\theta=s+1
and
s^{2}\mid k_{0}-s-1
. If
\theta^{\prime}\neq\theta
, then
s^{2}\mid k_{0}-2
and
s^{2}\mid k_{0}-s-1
, a contradiction. Thus, one of the following holds:

(i)

each line of
PG_{c-1}(s)
intersects
B
in at most
2
points, and hence
B
is a
k_{0}
-cap of
PG_{c-1}(s)
(e.g. see
[
28
, Section 27]
for a definition of cap);

(ii)

each line of
PG_{c-1}(s)
intersecting
B
in at least two distinct points is contained in
B
, and hence B a
(j-1)
-dimensional subspace of
PG_{c-1}(s)
for some integer
j\geqslant 2
.

Assume that (i) occurs. The group
H_{x,y}
contains a subgroup
X:R
with
X
elementary abelian of order
s^{2(c-1)}
containing
X
and
R\cong SL_{c-2}(s)
.The group
X:R

fixes
\ell
pointwise,
R
permutes
2
-transitively the pencil
\mathcal{F}
of planes of
PG_{c-1}(s)
containing
\ell
, and for any
\pi\in\mathcal{F}
, and the group
X
induces the full
(\ell,\ell)
-elation
group of
\pi
. Note that,
\left|X:X_{B}\right|=s
since
\Gamma_{x}
acts transitively on the set of blocks of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
containing

x
,

r_{0}=\frac{s\left(s^{c-1}-1\right)}{\left(s-1\right)(k_{0}-1)}\cdot\frac{s^{c-2}-1}{s-1}

and
s^{2}\mid k_{0}-2
. Therefore
\left|X:X_{B}\right|\mid s
,
and hence
\left|X:X_{B}X_{(\pi)}\right|\mid s
. Thus
\left|X^{\pi}:E\right|\mid s
with
E=\left(X_{B}\right)^{\pi}
a
subgroup of the full
(\ell,\ell)
-elation group
X_{\pi}^{\pi}
of
\pi
. In particular,
s\mid\left|E\right|
. Since
\mathcal{F}

provides a partition of
PG_{c-1}(s)\setminus\ell
and
k_{0}\geqslant 3
, we
may choose
\pi
such that
\left|\pi\cap B\right|\geqslant 3
. Thus
E
is group of
(\ell,\ell)
-elations of
\pi
of order divisible by
s
, and
\pi\cap B
is an
E
-invariant arc of
\pi
.

Let
w\in\pi\cap B
be such that
w\neq x,y
, then
w\notin\ell
since
\pi\cap B
is an arc of
\pi
. If
E(x,\ell)\neq 1
, let
m
be the line
of
\pi
containing
x
and
w
, and let
\tau\in E
be any nontrivial
(x,\ell)
-elation of
\pi
contained in
\ell
. Such a
\tau
does exist
since
E
is the
(\ell,\ell)
elation group of
\pi
. Thus
w^{\tau}\in m\setminus\left\{x,w\right\}
. Moreover,
w^{\tau}\in B
since
E=X_{\pi}^{\pi}
and
X_{\pi}\leqslant H_{x,y,B}
. So
\left\{x,w,w^{\tau}\right\}\subseteq\pi\cap m
with
\left|\left\{x,w,w^{\tau}\right\}\right|=3
, whereas
B
is a
k_{0}
-cap. Thus
E(x,\ell)=1
, and
similarly
E(y,\ell)=1
.

Let
u\in\ell\setminus\left\{x,y\right\}
such that
E(u,\ell)\neq 1
.
Clearly,
w^{E(u,\ell)}\subset\pi\cap B
with
w^{E(u,\ell)}
is
contained in a line
n
of
\pi
and
\left|w^{E(u,\ell)}\right|=\left|E(u,\ell)\right|
. Therefore
\left|E(u,\ell)\right|=2
, and hence
s
is even. Then
k_{0}
is even since
s^{2}\mid k_{0}-2
. However, this is impossible since
k_{0}\mid v_{0}
and
v_{0}=\frac{s^{c}-1}{s-1}
.

Assume that (ii) occurs. Then

\displaystyle\frac{s^{c-2}-1}{s-1}=\lambda={c-2\brack j-2}_{s}\text{,}

and hence
j=3
and
c\geqslant 4
, since
\Gamma
acts transitively on the set of all
(j-1)
-dimensional subspaces of
PG_{c-1}(s)
and
\lambda
is a prime and
j\geqslant 2
.
∎

Remark 5.9
.

The proof of Proposition
5.8
still works if we replace the assumption
\lambda>\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}
with
\mathcal{D}_{1}
being of type Ia. Indeed, if
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia, then
\lambda\mid s^{i}-1
,
c>i
and
\lambda
fulfills (
3.21
) by Theorem
3.9
.

Summarizing the previous results, we obtain the following theorem.

Theorem 5.10
.

Assume that Hypothesis
5.2
holds. Then one of the following holds:

(1)

\mathcal{D}_{0}\cong AG_{c/h}(s^{h})
,
c/h\geqslant 3
, with all affine planes as blocks and
\lambda=\frac{s^{c-h}-1}{s^{h}-1}
, and either
SL_{c/h}(s^{h})\unlhd\Gamma_{x}
or
(c,h,s)=(4,1,2)
and
A_{7}\unlhd\Gamma_{x}
.

(2)

\mathcal{D}_{0}\cong PG_{c-1}(s)
,
c\geqslant 4
, with all planes as blocks and
\lambda=\frac{s^{c-2}-1}{s-1}
, and
PSL_{c}(s)\unlhd G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}\leqslant P\Gamma L_{c}(s)
,
c\geqslant 3
.

Proof.

The assertion immediately follows from Propositions
5.6
and
5.8
.
∎

Finally, we are in position to prove Theorem
5.1
.

Proof of Theorem
5.1
.

Assume that
\lambda>\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}
. Then
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia and
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a
2
-
(v_{0},k_{0},\lambda)
design admitting
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
as a flag-transitive automorphism group by Theorem
4.1
(1). Further Hypothesis
5.2
holds, and hence
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is as in Theorem
5.10
.

Assume that
\mathcal{D}_{0}\cong AG_{c/h}(s^{h})
,
c/h\geqslant 3
, with all affine planes as blocks and
\lambda=\frac{s^{c-h}-1}{s^{h}-1}
. Then
\lambda\mid As^{c}+s^{2h}
since
\lambda\mid k_{1}
by Theorem
3.9
and
k_{1}=A\frac{v_{0}}{k_{0}}+1
by Proposition
3.5
(3). Therefore
\lambda\mid As^{h}(s^{c-h}-1)+(A+s^{h})s^{h}
, and hence
\lambda\mid A+s^{h}
since
\lambda=\frac{s^{c-h}-1}{s^{h}-1}
is a prime number. However, this is impossible since
\lambda\geqslant A(s^{2h}-1)+1
with
A\geqslant 1
by Proposition
3.5
(4).

Assume that
\mathcal{D}_{0}\cong PG_{c-1}(s)
,
c\geqslant 4
, with all planes as blocks and
\lambda=\frac{s^{c-2}-1}{s-1}
. Then
\lambda\mid A\frac{s^{c}-1}{s-1}+(s^{2}+s+1)
since
\lambda\mid k
, and hence
\lambda\mid As^{2}\frac{s^{c-2}-1}{s-1}+A(s+1)+(s^{2}+s+1)
. Therefore,
\lambda\mid A(s+1)+(s^{2}+s+1)
since
\lambda=\frac{s^{c-2}-1}{s-1}
. On the other hand,
\lambda\geqslant A(s^{2}+s)+1
by Proposition
3.5
(4). Thus
A(s^{2}+s)+1\leqslant A(s+1)+(s^{2}+s+1)
, which implies either
A=1
and
\frac{s^{c-2}-1}{s-1}\mid s^{2}+2s+2
, or
A=s=2
and
2^{c-2}-1=\lambda=13
. Both cases are clearly impossible since
s\geqslant 2
and
c\geqslant 4
. Thus, we have proven that
\lambda>\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}
leads to a contradiction. Therefore
\lambda\leqslant\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}
, and hence assertions (1)–(4) follow from (2.a)–(2.c) of Theorem
4.1
. Finally, assertion (5) follows from Lemma
2.5
. This completes the proof.
∎

6.
Further Reductions

In this section, we collect some additional constraints on the pairs
(\mathcal{D}_{1},G^{\Sigma})
that will play a central role in the completion of Theorem
1.1
. More precisely, we show that either the action of
G
on
\Sigma
is faithful, or
Soc(G_{\Delta}^{\Delta})
is an elementary abelian
l
-group for some
prime
l
acting point-regularly on
\mathcal{D}_{0}
, and
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia with
\eta=\frac{v_{0}}{k_{0}}
. Also, in the second case, we prove that
\mathcal{D}_{0}\cong PG_{c-1}(s)
with all planes as blocks and
\lambda=\frac{s^{c-2}-1}{s-1}
when
PSL_{c}(s)\trianglelefteq G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}\leqslant P\Gamma L_{c}(s)
,
c\geqslant 5
, and
v_{0}\leqslant s\frac{s^{c}-1}{s-1}\frac{s^{c-1}-1}{s-1}
.

Lemma 6.1
.

If
G_{(\Sigma)}\neq 1
, then the following hold:

(1)

Soc(G_{\Delta}^{\Delta})
is an elementary abelian
l
-group for some
prime
l
acting point-regularly on
\mathcal{D}_{0}
;

(2)

\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia with
\eta=\frac{v_{0}}{k_{0}}
,
k_{1}=\lambda
,
r_{1}=\frac{v_{1}-1}{\lambda-1}\lambda
and
b_{1}=\frac{v_{1}(v_{1}-1)}{\lambda-1}
.

Proof.

Assume that
G_{(\Sigma)}\neq 1
. Clearly,
G_{(\Sigma)}\leqslant G_{\Delta}
for each
\Delta\in\Sigma
. If there is
\Delta^{\prime}\in\Sigma
such that
G_{(\Sigma)}\leqslant G_{(\Delta^{\prime})}
, then
G_{(\Sigma)}\leqslant G_{(\Delta)}
since
G_{(\Sigma)}\unlhd G
and
G
acts transitively on
\Sigma
. This forces
G_{(\Sigma)}=1
, contrary to our assumption. Thus,
G_{(\Sigma)}^{\Delta}\neq 1
for each
\Delta\in\Sigma
, and hence
Soc(G_{\Delta}^{\Delta})\unlhd G_{(\Sigma)}^{\Delta}
for each
\Delta\in\Sigma
by Theorem
[
19
, Theorem 4.3B]
. Further,

(6.1)

\left|(B\cap\Delta)^{G_{(\Sigma)}^{\Delta}}\right|=\left|(B\cap\Delta)^{G(\Delta)G(\Sigma)}\right|=\left|(B\cap\Delta)^{G(\Sigma)}\right|\leqslant\left|B^{G_{(\Sigma)}}\right|

since
G_{(\Sigma),B}\leqslant G_{(\Sigma),B\cap\Delta}
. On the other hand, if
C\in B^{G_{(\Sigma)}}
then
C\cap\Delta^{\prime}=B\cap\Delta^{\prime}
for each
\Delta^{\prime}\in\Sigma
. Hence,
C^{\Sigma}=B^{\Sigma}
and so
\left|B^{G_{(\Sigma)}}\right|\leqslant\eta
. Then
\left|(B\cap\Delta)^{G_{(\Sigma)}^{\Delta}}\right|\leqslant\eta
by (
6.1
).

Let
S=Soc(G_{\Delta}^{\Delta})
, then
S
is either an
elementary abelian group for some prime
l
or a non-abelian simple group. Indeed, the previous assertion follows from the
2
-transitivity of
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
for
k_{0}=2
, from
[
8
, Main Theorem]
for
k_{0}\geqslant 3
and
\mu=\lambda
, and from
[
63
, Theorem 1]
for
k_{0}\geqslant 3
and
\mu=1
.

Assume
S
is non-abelian simple. Then

\left|(B\cap\Delta)^{G_{(\Sigma)}^{\Delta}}\right|=\frac{\left|(B\cap\Delta)^{S}\right|}{h}

with
h_{0}=\left|\left(G_{(\Sigma)}^{\Delta}\right)_{B\cap\Delta}:S_{B\cap\Delta}\right|
a divisor of the
order of
\left|Out(S)\right|
. Further,

\left|(B\cap\Delta)^{S}\right|=\frac{b_{0}}{h_{1}}
with
h_{1}=\left|\left(G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}\right)_{B\cap\Delta}:S_{B\cap\Delta}\right|
since
S
is normal in
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
, and the latter acts block-transitively on
\mathcal{D}_{0}
.
Therefore,

\frac{b_{0}}{h_{0}h_{1}}=\left|(B\cap\Delta)^{G_{(\Sigma)}^{\Delta}}\right|\leqslant\eta\leqslant\frac{v_{0}}{k_{0}}

Thus,
r_{0}\leqslant h
with
h=h_{0}h_{1}
since
b_{0}=v_{0}r_{0}/k_{0}
and

v_{0}-1<\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\lambda=r_{0}\leqslant\left|Out(S)\right|^{2}

since
\lambda>k_{0}-1
. Then

(6.2)

P(S)\leqslant v_{0}\leqslant\left|Out(S)\right|^{2}\text{,}

where
P(S)
denotes the minimal degree of the nontrivial transitive permutation representations of
S
. Then
S
is neither
A_{m}
with
m\geqslant 5
and
m\neq 6
, nor
S
is sporadic by
[
13
]
since
P(S)\geqslant 5
and
\left|Out(S)\right|=2
. Finally, it is a routine exercise showing that
S\cong PSL_{2}(9)
or
PSL_{3}(4)
by using
[
54
,
55
,
56
]
and
[
34
, Theorem 5.2.2]
according as
S
is a simple exceptional group of Lie type or a simple classical group, respectively. Then either
S\cong PSL_{2}(9)
,
\lambda=5
and
v_{0}\leqslant 16
, or
S\cong PSL_{3}(4)
,
\lambda=5
or
7
and
v_{0}\leqslant 144
. Therefore
v_{0}=6,10,15
or
21,56,102
, respectively, by
[
13
]
. Then
\lambda\mid v_{0}(v_{0}-1)
in both cases, and hence
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ic by Theorem
5.1
. However, this impossible since
v_{0}=16
or
29
according as
\lambda=5
or
7
, respectively, by Theorem
3.9
.

Assume that
S
is an elementary abelian group for some
prime
l
. Then

(6.3)

\eta\geqslant\left|B^{G_{(\Sigma)}}\right|=\left|G_{(\Sigma)}:G_{(\Sigma),B}\right|\geqslant\left|G_{(\Sigma)}:G_{(\Sigma),B\cap\Delta}\right|

since
G_{(\Sigma),B}\leqslant G_{(\Sigma),B\cap\Delta}
. Clearly,
G_{(\Sigma),B\cap\Delta}\leqslant G_{(\Sigma)}
implies
G_{(\Delta)}\cap G_{(\Sigma),B\cap\Delta}\leqslant G_{(\Delta)}\cap G_{(\Sigma)}
.
Conversely, if
\beta\in G_{(\Delta)}\cap G_{(\Sigma)}
then
\beta

fixes
\Delta
pointwise and hence
\beta
preserves
B\cap\Delta
. Thus

\beta\in G_{B\cap\Delta}
, and hence
\beta\in G_{(\Delta)}\cap G_{(\Sigma),B\cap\Delta}
since
G_{(\Sigma),B\cap\Delta}=G_{(\Sigma)}\cap G_{B\cap\Delta}
. Thus
G_{(\Delta)}\cap G_{(\Sigma)}=G_{(\Delta)}\cap G_{(\Sigma),B\cap\Delta}
, and hence

(6.4)

\left|G_{(\Sigma)}:G_{(\Sigma),B\cap\Delta}\right|=\left|G_{(\Delta)}G_{(\Sigma)}:G_{(\Delta)}G_{(\Sigma),B\cap\Delta}\right|=\left|G_{(\Sigma)}^{\Delta}:G_{(\Sigma),B\cap\Delta}^{\Delta}\right|\geqslant\left|S:S_{B\cap\Delta}\right|

since
G_{(\Sigma),B\cap\Delta}^{\Delta}=\left(G_{(\Sigma)}^{\Delta}\right)_{B\cap\Delta}
and
S\unlhd G_{(\Delta)}^{\Sigma}
.
Note that, the blocks of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
are subspaces of
AG_{d}(p)
. Indeed, the proof of Lemma
5.5
(1) does not make use of the assumption
\lambda>\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}
, and hence it still works under the Hypothesis
3.12
, but it makes use of the fact
\lambda\nmid v_{0}
, which we may still assume it since
\mathcal{D}
is of type Ia or Ic by Theorem
5.1
.

Then
\left|S:S_{B\cap\Delta}\right|=\frac{v_{0}}{k_{0}}
since
S
is the
translation group of
AG_{d}(p)
. Combining (
6.3
) with (
6.4
), and bearing in mind that
\eta\mid\frac{v_{0}}{k_{0}}
, we obtain

\eta=\frac{v_{0}}{k_{0}}
. Then
r_{1}=\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\lambda
and
b_{1}k_{1}=v_{0}\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\lambda
, and hence
b_{1}\frac{k_{1}}{\lambda}=v_{1}\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}
. Then
(k_{1}-1)\cdot\frac{k_{1}}{\lambda}\cdot b_{1}=v_{1}\cdot(v_{1}-1)
by Lemma
6.1
(2). If
\frac{k_{1}}{\lambda}\geqslant 2
, then
2(k_{1}-1)\leqslant v_{1}-1
since
b_{1}\geqslant v_{1}
. So
2k_{1}-1<v_{1}<2k_{1}
, which is a contradiction. Thus
k_{1}=\lambda
, and hence
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia by Theorem
3.9
.
∎

Proposition 6.2
.

Assume that Hypothesis
3.12
holds. If
PSL_{c}(s)\trianglelefteq G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}\leqslant P\Gamma L_{c}(s)
,
c\geqslant 5
, and
v_{0}\leqslant s\frac{s^{c}-1}{s-1}\frac{s^{c-1}-1}{s-1}
, then
\mathcal{D}_{0}\cong PG_{c-1}(s)
with all planes as blocks and
\lambda=\frac{s^{c-2}-1}{s-1}
.

Proof.

Let
S
be the subgroup of
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
isomorphic to
PSL_{c}(s)
. Then
S
acts point-transitively on
\mathcal{D}_{0}
since
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
acts point-primitively on
\mathcal{D}_{0}
, and hence
v_{0}=\left|S:S_{x}\right|
. It follows that

\left|S_{x}\right|\geqslant\frac{\left|S\right|}{s\frac{s^{c}-1}{s-1}\frac{s^{c-1}-1}{s-1}}=\frac{s^{c(c-1)/2-1}(s-1)^{2}}{(c,s-1)}\prod_{j=2}^{c-2}\left(s^{j}-1\right)\geqslant s^{c(c-1)/2-1}\prod_{j=1}^{c-2}\left(s^{j}-1\right)\text{,}

and hence
\left|S_{x}\right|^{2}>s^{2c^{2}-4c-2}>s^{c^{2}-1}>\left|S\right|
since
c\geqslant 5
. Let
M

be a maximal subgroup of
S
containing
S_{x}
. Then
\left|S\right|<\left|S_{x}\right|^{2}\leqslant\left|M\right|^{2}
. If
M
is not a geometric subgroup of
S
,
then
c=5
,
s=3
and
M\cong M_{11}
by
[
1
, Table 7]
since
c\geqslant 5
.
Note that,
\lambda
divides
\left|S_{x}\right|
since
\lambda

divides
\left|S\right|
but not
v_{0}
. Then
\lambda
divides
\left|M\right|
and so
\lambda=5
or
11
since
\lambda>3
,
and we reach a contradiction since
v_{0}\leqslant 2\cdot 11^{2}(11-1)=\allowbreak 2420
, whereas
\left|S:M\right|=30023136

divides
v_{0}
. Thus
M
is a geometric subgroup of
S
, and hence
M
is
one of the groups listed in
[
1
, Proposition 4.7]
. Now, it is a routine
exercise checking which groups in the previous list fulfills
S<\left|M\right|^{2}
, and we obtain that either
M
is a maximal parabolic
subgroup of
S
of type
P_{m}
with
m\leqslant c/2
or
M
is a
\mathcal{C}_{3}
-subgroup of
S
of type
GL_{c/2}(s^{2})
or a
\mathcal{C}_{8}
-subgroup
of
S
of type
Sp_{c}(s)
or
U_{c}(s^{1/2})
.

If
M
is either a
\mathcal{C}_{3}
-subgroup of type
GL_{c/2}(s^{2})
, or
a
\mathcal{C}_{8}
-subgroup of
S
of type
Sp_{c}(s)
or
U_{c}(s^{1/2})
. Then either
\left|M\right|\leqslant 2s^{c^{2}/2-2}
or
\left|M\right|\leqslant 4s^{c(c+1)/2}
, or
\left|M\right|<2s^{c^{2}/2}
, respectively, (see
[
40
, pp. 34–36]
). Thus either
v_{0}\geqslant s^{c^{2}/2}/2
or
v_{0}\geqslant s^{(c^{2}-c-4)/2}
or
v_{0}\geqslant s^{c^{2}/2-2}
, respectively, since
\left|S\right|\geqslant s^{c^{2}-2}
. Then
v_{0}\geqslant s^{c^{2}/2-2}
in each case, and hence
s^{c^{2}/2-2}\leqslant s\frac{s^{c}-1}{s-1}\frac{s^{c-1}-1}{s-1}<2s^{2c-1}
. However, this is impossible
since
c\geqslant 5
and
s\geqslant 2
. Thus,
M
is a maximal parabolic subgroup of
S
of type
P_{m}
. Then either
S_{x}=M
is of type
P_{m}
, or
S_{x}
is
of type
P_{m,c-m}
and
M
is of type
P_{m}
or
P_{c-m}
by
[
34
, Table
3.5.H]
for
c\geqslant 13
and
[
7
, Section 8]
for
5\leqslant c\leqslant 13
.

Assume that
S_{x}
is
of type
P_{m,c-m}
and
M
is of type
P_{m}
or
P_{c-m}
. By
[
34
, Proposition 4.1.22(II)]
, one has

v_{0}={c\brack c-m}_{s}\cdot{c-m\brack m}_{s}=\frac{\prod_{j=1}^{c}\left(s^{j}-1\right)}{\left(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\left(s^{j}-1\right)\right)^{2}\cdot\left(\prod_{j=1}^{c-2m}\left(s^{j}-1\right)\right)}\text{,}

and by using
[
1
, Lemma 4.1(i)]
, we deduce

v_{0}>\frac{(1-s-s^{-2})s^{c(c+1)/2}}{(1-s^{-1})^{3}(1-s^{-2})^{3}s^{m(m+1)+(c-2n)(c-2m+1)/2}}>s^{m(2c-3m)}\text{.}

On the other hand,
v_{0}\leqslant s\frac{s^{c}-1}{s-1}\frac{s^{c-1}-1}{s-1}
implies
v_{0}\leqslant 2s^{2c-2}
. Hence
s^{m(2c-3m)}<2s^{2c-2}
, from which we derive
m=1
and

v_{0}=\frac{(s^{c}-1)(s^{c-1}-1)}{(s-1)^{2}}

since
c\geqslant 5
. Let
l
be the prime dividing
s
. Then the highest power of
l
dividing
v_{0}-1
is
s
,
2s
or
2^{c-1}
according as
s
is odd,
s
is even and
s>2
or
s=l=2
, respectively. Moreover, as pointed out in
[
48
, p. 339, (a)]
, there is a
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
-orbit of length a power of
l
. Then either
\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\mid s
for
s
odd, or
\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\mid 2s
for
s

even and
s>2
, or
\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\mid s^{c-1}
for
s=2
by Lemma
2.5
(2). Then either
\left(\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\right)^{2}<4s^{2}
or
\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\mid s^{c-1}
according as
s>2
or
s=2
, respectively. Assume that the
former occurs. Then
k_{0}<v_{0}
,
3<v_{0}
,
k_{0}-1\mid v_{0}-1
and Proposition
3.5
(4) imply
\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\geqslant 2>\frac{v_{0}}{v_{0}-1}\cdot\frac{k_{0}-1}{\lambda}
. Therefore
\left(\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\right)^{2}>\frac{v_{0}}{\lambda}
, and hence
4s^{2}>\frac{s^{c}-1}{s-1}
since
\lambda\leqslant\frac{s^{c-2}-1}{s-1}
, being
\lambda\nmid v_{0}
, which is not the case. Hence
s=2
,
\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}=2^{x}
with
1<x\leqslant c-1
,
v_{0}=(2^{c}-1)(2^{c-1}-1)
and
k_{0}=2^{c-1-x}(2^{c}-3)+1
. We know that
\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\geqslant\lambda>k_{0}
y Theorem
5.1
. Thus
2^{x}>2^{c-1-x}(2^{c}-3)+1>2^{2(c-1)-x}
since
c\geqslant 5
, and hence
x>c-1
, a contradiction.

Assume that
S_{x}=M
is of type
P_{m}
with
m\leqslant c/2
. Then

s^{m(c-m)}\leqslant{c\brack m}_{s}=v_{0}\leqslant(s^{c}-1)s\frac{s^{c-2}-1}{s-1}<s^{2c-1}\text{,}

and hence
m\geqslant 3
. If
m=3
, then

\frac{(s^{c}-1)(s^{c-1}-1)(s^{c-2}-1)}{(s-1)(s^{2}-1)(s^{3}-1)}\leqslant(s^{c}-1)s\frac{s^{c-2}-1}{s-1}

and hence
m=3
and
c=6
since
m\leqslant c/2
. Then

v_{0}=(s^{2}+1)(s^{3}+1)\left(s^{4}+s^{3}+s^{2}+s+1\right)

and hence
\lambda
divides
s-1
or
s^{2}+s+1
since
\lambda\nmid v_{0}(v_{0}-1)
. However, this is contrary to
v_{0}<k<2\lambda(\lambda^{2}-1)
.

Suppose that
m=2
. Then

v_{0}=\frac{(s^{c}-1)(s^{c-1}-1)}{(s^{2}-1)\left(s-1\right)}\text{.}

Further, since
S
is a rank
3
group with subdegrees
s(s+1)\frac{s^{c-2}-1}{s-1}
and
\frac{s^{4}(s^{c-2}-1)(s^{c-3}-1)}{(s^{2}-1)\left(s-1\right)}
, it follows that
\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}
divides
s\frac{(s^{c-2}-1)}{s^{2}-1}
or
s\frac{(s^{c-2}-1)}{s^{2}-1}(s+1)\left(s+1,\frac{c-3}{2}\right)

according as
c
is even or odd respectively. Therefore,

\frac{\frac{(s^{c}-1)(s^{c-1}-1)}{(s^{2}-1)\left(s-1\right)}-1}{k_{0}-1}\mid s\frac{(s^{c-2}-1)}{s^{2}-1}\alpha

with
\alpha=1
or
(s+1)\left(s+1,\frac{c-3}{2}\right)
, respectively, and
hence

(6.5)

\frac{s^{c}-1}{s-1}+s\mid\alpha(k_{0}-1)\text{.}

Then

(6.6)

\frac{s^{c}+s^{2}-s-1}{s-1}<\alpha k_{0}<\alpha\lambda\leqslant\alpha\frac{s^{c-2}-1}{s-1}\text{,}

and hence
\alpha>1
. Therefore,
c
is odd and
\alpha\mid(s+1)\left(s+1,\frac{c-3}{2}\right)
.
If
\alpha\leqslant\frac{(s+1)^{2}}{2}
, then (
8.7
) leads to
s^{c-2}(s^{2}-2s-1)+(3s^{2}-1)\leqslant 0
and hence to
s=2
and
\alpha\leqslant 5/2
. Therefore, (
6.5
) becomes
2^{c}+1\mid\alpha(k_{0}-1)
. Actually,
\alpha=3
since
\alpha\mid 3\left(3,\frac{c-3}{2}\right)
and
\alpha>1
. So
2^{c}+1\leqslant 3(2^{c-2}-1)
, a contradiction. Thus,
\alpha=(s+1)^{2}
. Now, if
\frac{s^{c}-1}{s-1}+s\leqslant\frac{1}{2}(s+1)^{2}(k_{0}-1)
, we obtain
s=2
and
\alpha=9
by the previous argument. Moreover,
c\equiv 0\pmod{3}
. Then
k_{0}=\frac{2^{c}+10}{9}
since
\frac{2^{c}+1}{9}\mid k_{0}-1
and
k_{0}<\lambda\leqslant 2^{c-2}-1
. Then
2^{c}+10\mid 3(2^{c}-1)(2^{c-1}-1)
since
k_{0}\mid v_{0}
. Then
2^{c}+10\mid 8\cdot 9\cdot 11
, and hence
c=1
or
5
, whereas
c\geqslant 5
. Therefore,
\alpha=(s+1)^{2}
and
\frac{s^{c}-1}{s-1}+s=(s+1)^{2}(k_{0}-1)
, and hence

k_{0}=\frac{s^{c}+s^{3}+2s^{2}-2s-2}{(s^{2}-1)(s-1)}\text{.}

Then
s^{c}+s^{3}+2s^{2}-2s-2\mid(s^{c}-1)(s^{c-1}-1)
since
k_{0}\mid v_{0}
, and hence

s^{c}+s^{3}+2s^{2}-2s-2\mid(s^{3}+2s^{2}-2s-1)(s^{3}+2s^{2}-s-1)\text{,}

and we reach a contradiction since
c\geqslant 5
and
c
is odd.

Finally, assume that
m=1
. Hence,
S_{x}=M
is of type
P_{1}
. As pointed out in Remark
5.9
, the conclusion of Proposition
5.8
still holds under the assumption that
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia. Thus,
\mathcal{D}_{0}\cong PG_{c-1}(s)
with all planes as blocks and
\lambda=\frac{s^{c-2}-1}{s-1}
since
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia.
∎

7.
Reduction to the case where
G^{\Sigma}
is almost simple

In this section we prove the following theorem. More precisely, we show that
G^{\Sigma}
cannot be of affine type. The remainder of the theorem immediately follows from Theorem
5.1
.

Theorem 7.1
.

Assume that Hypothesis
3.12
holds. Then
\lambda\mid k_{1}
,
k_{0}<\lambda<\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}
and the following hold:

(1)

\mathcal{D}_{1}
is
2
-
(v_{1},k_{1},\lambda_{1})
design of type Ia or Ic admitting
G^{\Sigma}
as a flag-transitive point-primitive automorphism group of almost simple type;

(2)

\left|G^{\Sigma}\right|<\left|G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}\right|^{2}
for any
\Delta\in\Sigma
;

(3)

\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}>v_{1}^{1/2}
.

(4)

If
e_{1},...,e_{z}
are the lengths of the point-
G^{\Sigma}_{\Delta}
-orbits on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
distinct from
\{\Delta\}
, then

(7.1)

\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}\mid\left(e_{1},...,e_{z},v_{1}-1,\left|T_{\Delta}\right|\cdot\left|Out(T)\right|\right)\text{.}

Throughout this section, we assume that
T=Soc(G^{\Sigma})
is an elementary abelian
p
-group for some prime
p
. The point set of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
can be
identified with
d
-dimensional
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-vector space
V
in a way
that
T
acts as the translation group and
G^{\Sigma}=T:G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}
with
G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}
an irreducibly subgroup of
GL_{d}(p)
since
G^{\Sigma}
acts point primitively on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
. Thus
v_{1}=p^{d}
, and hence
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia by Theorems
3.9
(2) and
5.1
(1).

We formalize the conditions we have been discussing in the following hypothesis which will be assumed throughout this section.

Hypothesis 7.2
.

Hypothesis
3.12
holds,
T=Soc(G^{\Sigma})
is an elementary abelian
p
-group for some prime
p
,
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia with
v_{1}=p^{d}
.

Let
n
be the minimal divisor of
d
such that
G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}
is an irreducible subgroup of
\Gamma L_{n}(q)
, where
q=p^{d/n}
.

Lemma 7.3
.

Assume that Hypothesis
7.2
holds. Then
n>1
.

Proof.

Assume that
n=1
. Then
G^{\Sigma}\leqslant A\Gamma L_{1}(p^{d})
, and hence
\lambda\mid d
since
\lambda
divides the order of
G^{\Sigma}
and
\lambda\nmid p^{d}(p^{d}-1)
by Theorem
3.9
since
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia. Then
p^{d}=v_{1}<v\leqslant 2\lambda^{2}(\lambda-1)\leqslant 2d^{2}(d-1)
by
[
17
, Theorem 1]
, and hence either
p=2
and
2\leqslant d\leqslant 11
or
p=3
and
d=3,4
. Actually, only
p=2
and
\lambda=d=5,7,11
are admissible since
\lambda\mid d
,
\lambda
is a prime and greater than
3
. Moreover, we have
G^{\Sigma}\cong A\Gamma L_{1}(2^{d})
. Since
v_{1}-1=A\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}
and
\lambda\geqslant A(k_{0}-1)+1
by Proposition
3.5
(3)–(4), and
v_{1}-1=31,127
or
23\cdot 89
, it follows that
A=1
,
k_{1}=\frac{v_{0}}{k_{0}}+1
and
\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}=2^{d}-1
. Hence, the following admissible cases:
