, Sections 4.2–4.3]
:

(1)

N
is either a
\mathcal{C}_{2}
-subgroup of type
O_{n/2}^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}(2)\wr Z_{2}
with
\varepsilon^{\prime}=\pm
or a
\mathcal{C}_{2}
-subgroup of type
O_{n/2}^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}(4)
with
\varepsilon^{\prime}=\pm
; and
2^{\frac{1}{8}n^{2}-2}\mid\left|K:N\right|
;

(2)

N
is either a
\mathcal{C}_{2}
-subgroup of type
GL_{n/2}(2)
or a
\mathcal{C}_{3}
-subgroup of type
GU_{n/2}(2)
, and
2^{n(n-2)/8-\log_{2}(2,n/2)\text{ }}\mid\left|K:N\right|
.

Therefore,
2^{n(n-2)/8-2\text{ }}
divides
\left|K:N\right|
in
any case. Then
2^{n(n-2)/8-2\text{ }}\mid v_{0}
, and hence

\displaystyle 2^{n(n-2)/8-2-1}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle v_{0}-1=\frac{(2^{n/2}+1)\left(2^{n/2-1}-1\right)\left(k_{0}-1\right)}{A}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle(2^{n/2}+1)\left(2^{n/2-1}-1\right)\lambda\leqslant(2^{n/2}+1)\left(2^{n-2}-1\right)

since
\lambda\leqslant 2^{n/2}+1
, and hence
n=14
since
n
is even and
n\geqslant 14
. This forces
\lambda=127
.
However, this case cannot occur since
\lambda\mid 2^{14}-1
and
2^{14}\mid v_{1}(v_{1}-1)
, whereas
\lambda\nmid v_{1}(v_{1}-1)
.

Assume that
N\in\mathcal{C}_{1}(K)
. Suppose that
N
is the stabilizer of
a non-singular
(i-1)
-subspace in
PG_{n-1}(2)
. Then
\allowbreak 2^{(in+1)/2}

divides
\left|K:N\right|
and hence
v_{0}
. Then

2^{(in+1)/2}\leqslant v_{0}-1\leqslant(2^{n/2}+1)\left(2^{n-2}-1\right)\text{,}

and so
i=1
or
2
. If
i=2
, then
n\leqslant 6
since

\displaystyle\frac{2^{n-2}(2^{n/2}-1)(2^{n/2-1}-1)}{3}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\frac{2^{n-2}(2^{n/2}-\varepsilon 1)(2^{n/2-1}+\varepsilon_{2}1)}{(2-\varepsilon_{1}1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|K:N\right|\leqslant v_{0}\leqslant(2^{n/2}+1)\left(2^{n-2}-1\right)+1

with
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}
by
[
34
, Proposition
4.1.6(II)]
, contrary to
n\geqslant 14
. Therefore
i=1
, and
K_{x}=N
by

[
34
, Tables 3.5.H–I]
, and hence
v_{0}=2^{n/2-1}(2^{n/2}-\varepsilon 1)
. Thus
v_{0}-1\mid(v_{1}-1)(k_{0}-1)
implies

\left(2^{n/2}\allowbreak+\varepsilon 1\right)\left(2^{n/2-1}\allowbreak-\varepsilon 1\right)\mid\left(2^{n/2}\allowbreak-\varepsilon 1\right)\left(2^{n/2-1}\allowbreak+\varepsilon 1\right)(k_{0}-1)

since
v_{0}-1=\left(2^{n/2}\allowbreak+\varepsilon 1\right)\left(2^{n/2-1}\allowbreak-\varepsilon 1\right)
and
v_{1}-1=\left(2^{n/2}\allowbreak-\varepsilon 1\right)\left(2^{n/2-1}\allowbreak+\varepsilon 1\right)
. So
v_{0}-1\mid k_{0}-1
, contrary to
k_{0}<v_{0}
. Therefore,
N
is a maximal parabolic subgroup of
T
of type
P_{h}
,
1\leqslant h\leqslant\left(n+\varepsilon 1-1\right)/2
, and hence

{\left(n+\varepsilon 1-1\right)/2\brack h}_{2}\cdot\prod_{j=0}^{h-1}(2^{\left(n-\varepsilon 1-1\right)/2-j}+1)\leqslant(2^{n/2}+1)\left(2^{n-2}-1\right)+1

by
[
34
, Proposition 4.1.19(II)]
. Then

2^{h(\left(n+\varepsilon 1-1\right)/2)-h)}\cdot\prod_{j=0}^{h-1}(2^{\left(n-1-1\right)/2-j}+1)\leqslant(2^{n/2}+1)\left(2^{n-2}-1\right)

forcing

since
n\geqslant 14
. Further,
T_{x}=N
by
[
34
, Tables 3.5.H–I]
. Therefore
v_{0}=(2^{n/2}-\varepsilon 1)(2^{n/2-1}+\varepsilon 1)
,
and hence

(2^{n/2}-\varepsilon 1)(2^{n/2-1}+\varepsilon 1)-1\mid\left(2^{n/2}-\varepsilon 1\right)\left(2^{n/2-1}+\varepsilon 1\right)(k_{0}-1)

since

and
v_{1}-1=\left(2^{n/2}\allowbreak-\varepsilon 1\right)\left(2^{n/2-1}+\varepsilon 1\right)
. So
v_{0}-1\mid k_{0}-1
, contrary to
k_{0}<v_{0}
. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 8.13
.

Claim 2
.

\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia,

,
\lambda\mid q-1
and

.

. One has

(2). Thus the order of

divisible by
\lambda>3
, it follows from

, Hauptsatz II.8.27]

is isomorphic to one of the groups

,

,

. If

(1).

or

since

.
In the former case, one has

and
\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}=\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}=10

by (

) since

and

. Then
25
divides

, a contradiction since

. Then

,

and

. Then

. Moreover, either

and

or

by Proposition

(4), respectively. Actually, only

occur by Proposition

(2). Then

acts
2
-transitively on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
by
[
31
, Corollary 4.2]
, a contradiction.

If
T_{\Delta}\cong PSL_{2}(q^{1/m})
or
PGL_{2}(q^{1/m})
with
m
prime, then

\frac{q(q^{2}-1)}{(2,q-1)}\leqslant q^{2/m}(q^{2/m}-1)^{2}\cdot(2,q-1)\cdot\log_{p}q

by Lemma
8.2
(1), and hence
m=2
. Thus either
T_{\Delta}\cong PGL_{2}(q^{1/2})
,
q
is odd and
v_{1}=\frac{q^{1/2}(q+1)}{2}
, or
v_{1}=q^{1/2}(q+1)
. In both cases,
\lambda\mid q-1
since
\lambda\nmid v{1}
by Theorem
3.9
, because
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia or Ic by Theorem
7.1
(1), and
\lambda\mid\left|T_{\Delta}\right|
. If
q
is odd, then
\lambda
divides either
\frac{q^{1/2}-1}{2}
or
\frac{q^{1/2}+1}{2}
since
\lambda
is an odd prime, and hence
\lambda\leqslant\frac{q^{1/2}+1}{2}
. Then

3\frac{q^{1/2}(q+1)}{2}\leqslant 3v_{1}\leqslant v\leqslant 2\lambda^{2}(\lambda-1)^{2}\leqslant\frac{(q^{1/2}+1)^{2}}{2}\cdot\frac{q^{1/2}-1}{2}

by Lemma
3.1
and
[
17
, Theorem 1]
, and no admissible cases occurs. Therefore,
q
is even and
v_{1}=q^{1/2}(q+1)
.
one has

(8.19)

\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}\mid\left(q^{1/2}(q+1)-1,q^{1/2}(q-1)\cdot 2\cdot\log_{p}q\right)

by Theorem
7.1
(4). Now
\left(q^{1/2}(q+1)-1,q^{1/2}(q-1)\right)
divides
q^{1/2}-1
, and
hence
\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}\mid(q^{1/2}-1)\cdot\log_{p}q
by (
8.19
). Therefore,

q^{1/2}(q+1)\leqslant(q^{1/2}-1)^{2}\cdot\log_{p}^{2}q

by Theorem
7.1
(3), and no admissible cases occur since
q^{1/2}>1
.

Finally, assume that
T_{\Delta}\cong[q]:Z_{\frac{q-1}{(2,q-1)}}
.
Thus
v_{1}=q+1
, and hence either
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia or Ic by Theorem
7.1
(1). In the latter case,
q=v_{1}=\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\lambda+1)(\lambda-2)
, which is impossible. Thus
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia, and hence
\lambda\mid q-1
since
\lambda\nmid v_{1}(v_{1}-1)
by Theorem
3.9
.

Claim 3
.

If
K
be the stabilizer in
T
of a block
B^{\Sigma}
of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
, then
p
divides the order of
K
.

Let
K
be the stabilizer in
T
of a block
B^{\Sigma}
of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
. We know that
\lambda\mid k_{1}
by Theorem
7.1
, hence
\lambda
divides the order of
G^{\Sigma}_{B^{\Sigma}}
since
G^{\Sigma}
acts flag-transitively on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
. Then
\lambda
divides the order of
K
by Lemma
8.2
(1) since
\left|G^{\Sigma}_{B^{\Sigma}}:K\right|\mid\left|Out(T)\right|
.

Suppose that
p
does not divide the order of
K
. Then either
K\leqslant D_{\frac{2(q-1)}{(2,q-1)}}
or
K\cong A_{5}
,
q\equiv 1\pmod{10}
and
\lambda=5
since
\lambda\mid q-1
and
\lambda

is a prime divisor of the order of
K
and
\lambda>3
. In the latter case,
3(q+1)\leqslant 3v_{1}\leqslant 200
by Lemma
3.1
and
[
17
, Theorem 1]
, and hence
q=11,21,31,41
or
61
since
q\equiv 1\pmod{10}
. Therefore,
K=G^{\Sigma}_{B^{\Sigma}}
and
PSL_{2}(q)\unlhd G^{\Sigma}\leqslant PGL(2,q)
. Hence,
b_{1}=\frac{q(q^{2}-1)}{60\alpha}
with
\alpha=1,2
. Also,
\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}=q
and so
A=1
since
v_{1}=q+1
and
v_{1}=A\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}+1
. Then
k_{1}=\frac{v_{0}}{k_{0}}+1
with
k_{1}\mid 60
and
2\leqslant k_{0}\leqslant 5
, and easy computations show that no
(q,k_{0},v_{0},k_{1},v_{1})=(11,5,45,10,12),(31,15,435,30,32)
, or
(61,30,1770,60,62)
. Then
r_{1}=\frac{33}{2\alpha},\frac{899}{2\alpha}
or
\frac{3599}{\alpha}
, respectively, and hence only
(q,k_{0},v_{0},k_{1},v_{1},r_{1})=(61,30,1770,60,62,3599)
is admissible. However, this remaining case is excluded since
r_{1}
is divisible by
59
, whereas the order of
PGL_{2}(61)
, and hence the order of
G^{\Sigma}
, is not.

Finally, assume that
K\leqslant D_{\frac{2(q-1)}{(2,q-1)}}
. Now,
K
contains a unique
cyclyc subgroup
C
of order a multiple of
\lambda
and a divisor of
q-1
. Furthermore,
C
is normal in
G_{B^{\Sigma}}^{\Sigma}
. Clearly, the actions
G^{\Sigma}
on the point set of
\mathcal{D}_{1}

and on
PG_{1}(q)
are equivalent. Hence,
C
a fixes exactly
two points of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and acts semiregularly elsewhere. Then
Fix(C)\cap B^{\Sigma}=\varnothing
, and hence
k_{1}=\frac{q-1}{y}
for some
y\geqslant 1
, since
G_{B^{\Sigma}}^{\Sigma}
acts transitively on
B^{\Sigma}
. Actually,
y=1
since
q+1=v_{1}<2k_{1}
. Then
b_{1}=\frac{q(q+1)}{2}
and hence
r_{1}=\frac{q^{2}-1}{2}
, whereas
\frac{r_{1}}{(r_{1},\lambda_{1})}=\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}
divides
q
and
\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}>\lambda>3
. Thus,
p

divides the order of
K
.

Claim 4
.

K\leqslant[q]:Z_{q-1}

Assume that
K
is not contained in
[q]:Z_{q-1}
. Then either
K
is
PSL_{2}(q^{1/h})
or
PGL_{2}(q^{1/h})
with
h>1
, or
K\cong A_{5}
when
q=3^{4u}
,
u\geqslant 1
and
\lambda=5
by
[
30
, Hauptsatz II.8.27]
since
\lambda\mid\left(q-1,\left|K\right|\right)
with
\lambda>3
by Claims 2 and 3 and
p
divides
\left|K\right|
by Claim 4. We may use the same argument used to rule out
A_{5}
in Claim 3, as it is is independent
from the divisibility of the order of
K
by
p
, to exclude
K\cong A_{5}
when
q=3^{4u}
,
u\geqslant 1
and
\lambda=5
. Thus,
K
is
PSL_{2}(q^{1/h})
or
PGL_{2}(q^{1/h})
with
h>1
. The actions of the actions
G^{\Sigma}
on the point set of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
on
PG_{1}(q)
are equivalent, hence the
K^{\prime}
-orbits on the point set of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
are one of length
q^{h}+1
, which is a copy of
PG_{1}(q^{1/h})
, one of length
q^{h}(q^{h}-1)
provided that
q^{1/h}+1\mid q-1
, and the remaining ones each of length
\frac{q^{h}(q^{2h}-1)}{(2,q^{h}-1)}
for
h>2
(for instance, see
[
10
, Lemma 14]
for
q
odd). Then the
G^{\Sigma}_{B^{\Sigma}}
-orbits on the point set of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
are one of length
q^{h}+1
, one of length
q^{h}(q^{h}-1)
provided that
q^{1/h}+1\mid q-1
, and the remaining ones each of length
\frac{q^{1/h}(q^{2/h}-1)}{(2,q^{1/h}-1)}\theta
with
\theta\mid(2,q-1)\cdot\log_{p}q
and
h>2
. Therefore, the possibility for
k_{1}
is one among
q^{1/h}+1
,
q^{1/h}(q^{1/h}-1)
when
q^{1/h}+1\mid q-1
, or
\frac{q^{1/h}(q^{2/h}-1)}{(2,q^{1/h}-1)}\theta
with
\theta\mid(2,q-1)\cdot\log_{p}q
since
B^{\Sigma}
is a point-
G^{\Sigma}_{B^{\Sigma}}
-orbit on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
. On the other hand,
k_{1}>v_{1}/2=(q+1)/2
which forces
q=4
for
k_{1}=q^{1/h}+1
, and
h=2
for
k_{1}=q^{1/h}(q^{1/h}-1)
. However, the former cannot occurs since
T\cong PSL_{2}(4)\cong A_{5}
is ruled out in Lemma
8.6
. The latter implies
k_{1}=q^{1/2}(q^{1/2}-1)
, and hence
v_{0}=(q-q^{1/2}-1)k_{0}
and
v_{0}=q(k_{0}-1)+1
. Then
k_{0}=q^{1/2}-1
and
v_{0}=(q-q^{1/2}-1)(q^{1/2}-1)
. Further,
\lambda\mid q^{1/2}-1
since
\lambda\mid k_{1}
by Theorem
7.1
and
\lambda\mid q-1
by Claim 2. So
\lambda\mid k_{0}
, which is contrary to Theorem
7.1
.

Assume that
k_{1}=\frac{q^{1/h}(q^{2/h}-1)}{(2,q^{1/h}-1)}\theta
with
\theta\mid(2,q-1)\cdot\log_{p}q
and
h>2
. Then either
\lambda\mid q^{1/h}-1
or
\lambda\mid q^{1/h}+1
since
\lambda\mid k_{1}
and
\lambda\mid q-1
. Then

(8.20)

3(q+1)\leqslant v\leqslant 2\lambda^{2}(\lambda-1)\leqslant 2q^{1/h}(q^{1/h}+1)^{2}

by Lemma
3.1
and
[
17
, Theorem 1]
. Then
h=3
and
q^{1/3}=2,3
or
4
. Actually, only
q^{1/3}=4
and
\lambda=5
are admissible since
\lambda\mid q^{1/h}-1
or
\lambda\mid q^{1/h}+1
and
\lambda
is a prime greater than
3
. However, this is contrary to
\lambda\mid q-1
. Thus, we obtain
K\leqslant[q]:Z_{q-1}
.

Claim 5
.

T
is not isomorphic to
PSL_{2}(q)
.

If follows from Claims 2 and 4 that,
\left|K\right|=p^{t}\frac{p^{(t,h)}-1}{e}
with
e\mid p^{(t,h)}-1
and
\lambda=\frac{p^{(t,h)}-1}{\theta}

with
\theta\mid e
. Moreover,
Fix(P)\cap B^{\Sigma}=\varnothing
, where
P
is the Sylow
p
-subgroup of
K
, as
G_{B^{\Sigma}}^{\Sigma}
acts transitively on
B^{\Sigma}
. Therefore,
p^{t}\frac{p^{(t,h)}-1}{\theta}\mid k_{1}
since
\lambda=\frac{p^{(t,h)}-1}{\theta}>3
and
\lambda\mid k_{1}
by Theorem
7.1
. Furthermore,
(t,h)\leqslant t<h
since
k_{1}<v_{1}
. If
(t,h)\leqslant h/3
, then
\lambda\leqslant q^{1/3}-1
, and we may use (
8.20
) to reach a contradiction. Therefore
h/3<(t,h)\leqslant t<h
, and hence
(t,h)=t=h/2
. Then
q^{1/2}\mid\frac{v_{0}}{k_{0}}+1
, and hence
q^{1/2}\mid v_{0}+k_{0}
. On the other hand,
v_{0}+k_{0}=q(k_{0}-1)+1+k_{0}
. Thus, we obtain
q^{1/2}\mid 1+k_{0}
. Then
\frac{q^{1/2}-1}{\theta}\geqslant\lambda\geqslant k_{0}\geqslant q^{1/2}-1
implies
\theta=1
and
\lambda=k_{0}=q^{1/2}-1
, contrary to Theorem
7.1
. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 8.14
.

Assume that Hypothesis
3.12
holds. Then
T\cong PSL_{n}(q)
and
v_{1}=\frac{q^{n}-1}{q-1}
. In particular,
T
acts point
2
-transitively on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
.

Proof.

We are going to show that
v_{1}>2\lambda^{2}
in each case as in of Table
23
, except that as in Line
1. Further
v_{0}>\lambda+1
since
\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}>\lambda
by Theorem
7.1
(3) and (
3.24
) and
k_{0}\geqslant 2
. This leads to
v>2\lambda^{2}(\lambda+1)
, contrary to
[
17
, Theorem 1]
, thus showing that only case as in line 1 of Table
23
holds, which is the assertion.
We provide details of computations in one case, namely that as in line 2 of Table
23
. The computations in the remaining cases are similar, and hence they are omitted. Now, assume that
T_{\Delta}
,
v_{1}
and
\lambda
are as in line 2 of Table
23
. One has

\displaystyle\frac{q^{n}-1}{q-1}\cdot\frac{q^{n-1}-1}{q^{2}-1}-2\left(\frac{q^{n-2}-1}{q-1}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\left(q^{n}-1\right)\left(q^{n-1}-1\right)-2\left(q^{n-2}-1\right)^{2}\left(q+1\right)}{(q-1)^{2}(q+1)}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle\frac{q^{n}(q^{n-4}\left(q^{3}-2q-2\right)-1)}{(q-1)^{2}(q+1)}>0

for
q>1
. Therefore,

\frac{q^{n}-1}{q-1}\cdot\frac{q^{n-1}-1}{q^{2}-1}>2\left(\frac{q^{n-2}-1}{q-1}\right)^{2}\geqslant 2\lambda^{2}\text{.}

Further
v_{0}>\lambda+1
since
\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}>\lambda
by
Theorem
7.1
(3) and (
3.24
) and
k_{0}\geqslant 2
. So
2\lambda^{2}(\lambda+1)<v_{1}v_{0}=v
, whereas
v\leqslant 2\lambda^{2}(\lambda-1)
by
[
17
, Theorem 1]
.

All cases of Table
23
, except that as in Line
1, are ruled out similarly. Thus, only case as in
line 1 occurs, which is the assertion.
∎

Lemma 8.15
.

Assume that Hypothesis
3.12
holds. Then
G_{(\Sigma)}=1
and
T\cong PSL_{n}(q)
with
n\geqslant 5
.

Proof.

Assume that
G_{(\Sigma)}\neq 1
. Then
(k_{1}-1)\cdot\frac{k_{1}}{\lambda}\cdot b_{1}=v_{1}\cdot(v_{1}-1)
by Lemma
6.1
(2). If
\frac{k_{1}}{\lambda}\geqslant 2
, then
2(k_{1}-1)\leqslant v_{1}-1
since
b_{1}\geqslant v_{1}
. So
2k_{1}-1<v_{1}<2k_{1}
, which is a contradiction. Thus
k_{1}=\lambda
. On the other hand,
\lambda\mid\left|T_{\Delta}\right|
by Lemma
8.2
(2), hence
\lambda\leqslant\frac{q^{n-1}-1}{q-1}
since
T\cong PSL_{n}(q)
and
\lambda
is a prime. Therefore,
\frac{q^{n}-1}{q-1}=v_{1}<2k_{1}=2\lambda\leqslant 2\frac{q^{n-1}-1}{q-1}
, which is not the case.

Assume that
n<5
. Then either
PSL_{3}(q)\trianglelefteq G\leqslant P\Gamma L_{3}(q)
and
v_{1}=q^{2}+q+1
, or
PSL_{4}(q)\trianglelefteq G\leqslant P\Gamma L_{4}(q)
and
v_{1}=q^{3}+q^{2}+q+1
by Lemmas
8.14
and
8.13
.

Assume that
PSL_{3}(q)\trianglelefteq G\leqslant P\Gamma L_{3}(q)
and
v_{1}=q^{2}+q+1
. Then
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ic by Theorem
3.9
since
v_{1}
is odd, and hence
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia by Theorem
7.1
. Further,
\lambda\nmid v_{1}(v_{1}-1)
by Theorem
3.9
. Then
\lambda\mid q-1
since
\lambda
divides the order of
\left|T_{\Delta}\right|
by Lemma
8.2
(2), and
A\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}=q(q+1)
by Proposition
3.5
(3).

Suppose that
p\nmid\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}
. Then
\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\mid q+1
. If
\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\leqslant\frac{q+1}{2}
then
2q\leqslant A\leqslant(v_{1}-1)^{1/2}\leqslant(q+1)^{1/2}
, a contradiction since
q\geqslant 2
. Thus,
\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}=q+1
and
A=q
since
\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}>\lambda>A
by Proposition
3.5
(4) and Theorem
7.1
. However, this impossible since
\lambda\mid q-1
.

Suppose that
p\mid\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}
. Then
\left(\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\right)_{p}\mid q
since
A\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}=q(q+1)
, and hence
(r_{1})_{p}\mid q
since
r_{1}=\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\cdot\frac{v_{0}}{k_{0}\eta}\cdot\lambda
with
\lambda\mid q-1
. If
\Delta
is any point of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
the
r_{1}
blocks of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
containing
\Delta
are partitioned in
T
-orbits of equal length since
T\unlhd G^{\Sigma}
and
G^{\Sigma}
acts flag-transitively on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
. Then
q^{2}\mid\left|T_{\Delta,B^{\Sigma}}\right|
, where
b^{\Sigma}
is any block of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
containing
\Delta
, since
q^{3}\mid\left|T_{\Delta}\right|
and
(r_{1})_{p}\mid q
. Consequently,
q^{2}\lambda\mid\left|T_{B^{\Sigma}}\right|
since
\lambda\mid(k_{1},q-1)
,
k_{1}\mid\left|G^{\Sigma}_{B^{\Sigma}}\right|
,
\left|G^{\Sigma}_{B^{\Sigma}}:T_{B^{\Sigma}}\right|\mid\left|Out(T)\right|
, and
\lambda\nmid\left|Out(T)\right|
by Lemma
8.2
(2). Further, one has
\left|T_{B^{\Sigma}}\right|\geqslant\frac{\left|G^{\Sigma}_{B^{\Sigma}}\right|}{3\cdot log_{p}q}\geqslant q^{2}\frac{q^{2}+q+1}{3\cdot log_{p}q}
, and hence
T_{B^{\Sigma}}
lies in the stabilizer in
T
of
a point or a line of of
PG_{2}(q)
by
[
24
,
39
]
. Therefore
b_{1}=e(q^{2}+q+1)
for some
e\geqslant 1
, and hence
k_{1}=\frac{r_{1}}{e}
since
b_{1}k_{1}=v_{1}r_{1}
and
v_{1}=q^{2}+q+1
. Thus,
k_{1}=\frac{A+k_{0}}{\alpha}\cdot\lambda
by (
3.21
) in Theorem
3.9
since
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia and Lemma
3.7
(2). Moreover,
\lambda=\frac{q-1}{\beta}
for some
\beta\geqslant 1
. Thus, we obtain

k_{1}=\frac{A+k_{0}}{\alpha}\cdot\frac{q-1}{\beta}\text{.}

If
A+k_{0}>\lambda
, then
A+k_{0}>\lambda\geqslant A(k_{0}-1)+1
by Proposition
3.5
(4), and hence either
k_{0}=2
and
\lambda=A+1
, or
k_{0}>2
and
A=1
. The latter implies
k_{0}+1>\lambda
, whereas
\lambda\geqslant k_{0}+1
by Theorem
3.9
since
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia. Thus
k_{0}=2
and
\lambda=A+1
. Then
A=\frac{q-1}{\beta}-1
since
\lambda=\frac{q-1}{\beta}
for some
\beta\geqslant 1
, and hence

\frac{q^{2}+q+1}{2}<k_{1}\leqslant\frac{1}{\alpha}\cdot\left(\frac{q-1}{\beta}+1\right)\cdot\left(\frac{q-1}{\beta}\right)

since
v_{1}<2k_{1}
. Thus
\alpha=\beta=1
, and hence
k_{1}=q(q-1)
, and so
(q-2)\frac{v_{0}}{2}+1=q(q-1)
since
k_{1}=A\frac{v_{0}}{k_{0}}+1
by Proposition
3.5
(3), and we reach a contradiction.

If
A+k_{0}\leqslant\lambda
, then

\frac{q^{2}+q+1}{2}<k_{1}\leqslant\frac{A+k_{0}}{\alpha}\cdot\lambda\leqslant\frac{\lambda}{\alpha}\cdot\frac{q-1}{\beta}\leqslant\frac{(q-1)^{2}}{\alpha\beta^{2}}\text{,}

forcing
\alpha=\beta=1
. Thus
\left(k_{1},\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}\right)=A+k_{0}
and
\lambda=q-1
is a Mersenne prime. Hence,
q=2^{t}

with
t
prime. Since
\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}>\lambda
by Theorem
7.1
, it follows that
v_{0}>(q-1)(k_{0}-1)+1
and hence

((q-1)(k_{0}-1)+1)q(q+1)<v_{0}v_{1}=v\leqslant 2\lambda^{2}(\lambda-1)=2(q-1)^{2}(q-2)

by
[
17
, Theorem 1]
. Thus
k_{0}=2
, and hence
v_{0}
is even since
k_{0}\mid v_{0}
. Then
v_{0}=q+2
and
A=q
since
A\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}=q(q+1)
and
q
is a power of
2
. Then
k_{1}=(A+k_{0})(q-1)=(q+2)(q-1)
and
k_{1}=A\frac{v_{0}}{k_{0}}+1=q\left(\frac{q}{2}+1\right)+1
, a contradiction.

Assume that
PSL_{4}(q)\trianglelefteq G\leqslant P\Gamma L_{4}(q)
and
v_{1}=q^{3}+q^{2}+q+1
. Suppose that
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ic. Then

(8.21)

q(q^{2}+q+1)=\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\lambda+1)(\lambda-2)

and hence there is a positive integer
\gamma
such that either
\lambda=q\gamma
, or
\lambda=q\gamma-1
, or
\lambda=q\gamma+2
. It is not difficult to see that, if we substitute any of these three possible values of
\lambda
in (
8.21
), no admissible solutions arise since
\lambda
is a prime number. Thus,
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ic by Theorem
7.1
(1). Then
\lambda\nmid v_{1}(v_{1}-1)
by Theorem
3.9
, and hence
\lambda=\frac{q-1}{e}
,
e\geqslant 1

since
\lambda
divides the order of
\left|T_{\Delta}\right|

by Lemma
8.2
(2). Then

3(q^{3}+q^{2}+q+1)<v\leqslant 2\lambda^{2}(\lambda-1)\leqslant 2\left(\frac{q-1}{e}\right)^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{q-1}{e}-1\right)

by Lemma
3.1
and
[
17
, Theorem 1]
, and we reach a contradiction. This completes the proof.
∎

Theorem 8.16
.

Assume that Hypothesis
3.12
holds. Then
T
cannot be non-abelian simple.

Proof.

We know that
PSL_{n}(q)\unlhd G\leqslant P\Gamma L_{n}(q)
,
n\geqslant 5
, acts in its
2
-transitive action of degree
\frac{q^{n}-1}{q-1}
on the point set of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
by Lemmas
8.14
and
8.15
, and
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of tyoe Ia or Ic by Theorem
7.1
. Further,
Soc(G_{\Delta}^{\Delta})
is either an elementary
abelian
u
-group for some prime
u
, or a non abelian simple group since
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
acts point-
2
-transitively on
\mathcal{D}_{0}
for
k_{0}=2
, by
[
9
, Theorem]
for
k_{0}>2
and
\mu=\lambda
, and by
[
63
, Theorem 1]
for
k_{0}>2
and
\mu=1
.
It follows that
Soc(G_{\Delta}^{\Delta})
is either an elementary
abelian
u
-group for some prime
u
, or a non abelian simple group in any case. Actually, either
Soc(G_{\Delta}^{\Delta})
an elementary abelian
p
-group of order
q^{n-1}
and
SL_{n-1}(q)\unlhd G_{x}^{\Delta}
, or
Soc(G_{\Delta}^{\Delta})\cong PSL_{n-1}(q)
in any case by
[
34
, Proposition 4.1.17(II)]
since
\lambda\nmid\left|Out(T)\right|
by Lemmas
8.2
(2) and
\lambda\nmid q-1
by
[
17
, Theorem 1]
for
n\geqslant 5
.

Assume that
Soc(G_{\Delta}^{\Delta})
is an elementary abelian
p
-group of order
q^{n-1}
and
SL_{n-1}(q)\unlhd G_{x}^{\Delta}
. Then
v_{0}=q^{n-1}
since
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
acts point-primitively on
\mathcal{D}_{0}
. If
k_{0}=2
, then
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
acts point-
2
-transitively on
\mathcal{D}_{0}
. Also,
q
is even since
k_{0}\mid v_{0}
. Then

\frac{q^{n}-1}{q-1}=A(q^{n-1}-1)+1

by Proposition
3.5
(3) since
v_{1}=\frac{q^{n}-1}{q-1}
, and hence
A=q
and
k_{1}=\frac{q^{n}}{2}+1
. Then
q=2
since
k_{1}<v_{1}
and
q
is even. Therefore,
v_{1}=2^{n}-1
,
k_{1}=2^{n-1}+1
,
r_{1}=(2^{n-1}-1)\cdot\frac{2^{n-2}}{\eta}\cdot\lambda
. Then
(k_{1},v_{1})\mid 3
by Lemma
3.7
(1), and hence
\lambda=\frac{2^{n-1}+1}{\theta}
with
\theta\mid 3
. However, this is impossible since
\lambda\mid 2^{n-i}-1
with
i\geqslant 1
since
\lambda\mid\left|T\right|
by Lemmas
8.2
(2) and
\lambda
is odd. Thus
k_{0}>2
, and hence
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is of type Ia by Theorem
7.1
(1).

Suppose that
\mu=\lambda
. Then
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a linear space, and hence
\mathcal{D}_{0}\cong AG_{n-1}(q)
with all the affine lines as blocks by
[
9
, Theorem]
since
SL_{n-1}(q)\unlhd G_{x}^{\Delta}
. In particular,
k_{0}=q
. Then

\frac{q^{n}-1}{q-1}=A\frac{q^{n-1}-1}{q-1}+1

by Proposition
3.5
(3). Thus,
A=1
, and hence
k_{1}=q^{n-1}+1
. Then
q=2
since
k_{1}<v_{1}
and
q
is even. Therefore,
v_{1}=2^{n}-1
,
k_{1}=2^{n-1}+1
,
r_{1}=(2^{n-1}-1)\cdot\frac{2^{n-2}}{\eta}\cdot\lambda
, and we reach a contradiction as above.

Suppose that
\mu=1
. Then
\mathcal{D}_{0}\cong AG_{n-1}(q)
,
n-1\geqslant 3
is even, with all affine planes as blocks and
\lambda=\frac{q^{n-2}-1}{q-1}
by Corollary
5.7
since
Soc(G_{\Delta}^{\Delta})
is an elementary abelian
p
-group of order
q^{n-1}
and
SL_{n-1}(q)\unlhd G_{x}^{\Delta}
. In particular,
k_{0}=q^{2}
. Then

\frac{q^{n}-1}{q-1}=A\frac{q^{n-1}-1}{q^{2}-1}+1\text{,}

and hence
A=q(q+1)
and
k_{1}=(q+1)q^{n-2}+1
. Then
\frac{q^{n-2}-1}{q-1}\mid q+2
since
\lambda=\frac{q^{n-2}-1}{q-1}
divides
k_{1}
by Theorem
7.1
, contrary to
n\geqslant 5
. Thus,
Soc(G_{\Delta}^{\Delta})
cannot be elementary abelian.

Finally, assume that
Soc(G_{\Delta}^{\Delta})\cong PSL_{n-1}(q)
,
n\geqslant 5
. If
k_{0}=2
, then
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
acts point-
2
-transitively on
\mathcal{D}_{0}
, and hence
v_{0}=\frac{q^{n-1}-1}{q-1}
since

n\geqslant 5
. Then
\frac{q^{n}-1}{q-1}=v_{1}=A(v_{0}-1)+1
implies
A\frac{q^{n-2}-1}{q-1}q\mid\frac{q^{n-1}-1}{q-1}q
, contrary to
n\geqslant 5
.

Assume that
k_{0}>2
and
\mu=\lambda
. Then
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a
linear space, and hence
\mathcal{D}_{0}\cong PG_{n-2}(q)
with al lines as
blocks by
[
9
, Theorem]
since
Soc(G_{\Delta}^{\Delta})\cong PSL_{n-1}(q)
,
n\geqslant 5
. Thus,
v_{0}=\frac{q^{n-1}-1}{q-1}
and
k_{0}=q+1
. Then

\frac{q^{n}-1}{q-1}=A\frac{q^{n-2}-1}{q-1}+1

and again a contradiction since
n\geqslant 5
.

Finally, assume that
k_{0}>2
and
\mu=1
. Note that,
v_{1}=A\frac{v_{0}-1}{k_{0}-1}+1
implies

v_{0}-1<v_{1}\frac{k_{0}-1}{A}<v_{1}\cdot\lambda\leqslant v_{1}\cdot k_{1}\leqslant v_{1}(v_{1}-1)\text{,}

and hence

v_{0}\leqslant q\cdot\frac{q^{n}-1}{q-1}\cdot\frac{q^{n-1}-1}{q-1}\text{.}

Then
\mathcal{D}_{0}\cong PG_{n-2}(q)
with
n
even and all planes as blocks and
\lambda=\frac{q^{n-3}-1}{q-1}
for
n-1\geqslant 5
by Proposition
6.2
. Thus
v_{0}=\frac{q^{n-1}-1}{q-1}
and
k_{0}=\frac{q^{3}-1}{q-1}
for
n\geqslant 6
, and hence

\frac{q^{n}-1}{q-1}=A\frac{q^{n-2}-1}{q^{2}-1}+1\text{,}

contrary to
n\geqslant 6
. Thus
n<6
, and hence
n=5
since
n\geqslant 5
. Then
\lambda\leqslant q^{2}+q+1
since
\lambda
is a prime such that
\lambda\nmid v_{1}
by Theorem
7.1
(1) and
\lambda\mid\left|T\right|
by Lemma
8.2
(2). If
q=2
, then
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
acts flag-transitively on a
\mathcal{D}_{0}
which is a
2
-design with
\lambda
prime, but this is contrary to
[
64
, Theorem 1]
since
Soc(G_{\Delta}^{\Delta})\cong PSL_{4}(2)\cong A_{8}
. Therefore
q>2
, and hence
v_{0}\geqslant\frac{q^{4}-1}{q-1}
by
[
34
, Theorem 5.2.2]
. So

\frac{q^{4}-1}{q-1}\cdot\frac{q^{5}-1}{q-1}\leqslant v_{0}v_{1}=v\leqslant 2\lambda^{2}(\lambda-1)\leqslant 2(q^{2}+q+1)^{2}(q^{2}+q)

by
[
17
, Theorem 1]
, which is not the case. This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
8.1
.

Let
\mathcal{D}=(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{B})
is a
2
-
(v,k,\lambda)
design with
\lambda
a prime number, and
G
is a
flag-transitive, point-imprimitive automorphism group of
\mathcal{D}
preserving a nontrivial
partition
\Sigma
of
\mathcal{P}
with
v_{1}
classes of size
v_{0}
. Possibly by substituting
\Sigma
with a
G
-invariant partition of
\mathcal{P}
finer that
\Sigma
, we may assume that
\Sigma
is minimal with respect to the ordering
\preceq
defined in the Subsection
2.1
. Then
\lambda\mid r
by Theorem
2.2
. Further, either
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is either symmetric
1
-design, or
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is a (possibly trivial)
2
-design by Theorem
2.4
(2). Assume that the latter occurs. Then
G^{\Sigma}
is an almost simple acting flag-transitively and point-primitively on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
by Theorem
7.1
(1). However, this is impossible Theorem
8.16
. Therefore
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is symmetric
1
-design with
k_{1}=v_{1}-1
by Theorem
2.4
, and hence the parameters for
\mathcal{D}_{0}
,
\mathcal{D}_{1}
, and
\mathcal{D}
are those recorded in Table
1
of Lemma
3.3
.
∎

9.
Completion of the proof of Theorem
1.1

This final small section is devoted to the completion of the proof of Theorem
1.1
. From Theorem
8.1
, we know that
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is a symmetric
1
-design with
k_{1}=v_{1}-1
. Thus
G^{\Sigma}
acts point-
2
-transitively on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
. We are going show that
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a translation plane and hence
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
is well known group of affine type. Finally, we get the conclusion of Theorem
1.1
by matching the parameters of
\mathcal{D}
with the possibilities for
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
and
G^{\Sigma}
, being
G
permutationally isomorphic to a subgroup of
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}\wr G^{\Sigma}
by
[
45
, Theorem 5.5]
.

Proposition 9.1
.

Assume that Hypothesis
2.6
holds. Then
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is a symmetric
1
-design with
k_{1}=v_{1}-1
, then
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a translation plane of order
k_{0}
and
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
is of affine type.

Proof.

It follows from Lemma
2.7
(2) and Lemma
3.3
that either
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a
2
-
(k_{0}^{2},k_{0},1)
design, or
\mu=1
,
\lambda_{0}=\lambda
,
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a
2
-
(k_{0}^{2},k_{0},\lambda)
design with
k_{0}\geqslant 3
and
r=r_{0}=(k_{0}+1)\lambda
. In the former case,
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a translation
plane of order
k_{0}
, and
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
is of affine type by
[
52
, Theorems 2 and
4]
since
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is an affine plane admitting
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
as a flag-transitive automorphism group, which is the assertion in this case.

Assume that
\mu=1
,
\lambda_{0}=\lambda
,
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a
2
-
(k_{0}^{2},k_{0},\lambda)
design with
k_{0}\geqslant 3
and
r=r_{0}=(k_{0}+1)\lambda
admitting
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
as a
flag-transitive automorphism group. If
\lambda=k_{0}
, then
\mathcal{D}
is a symmetric
2
-design by Lemma
3.3
. Then
\mathcal{D}_{0}\cong AG_{2}(2)
or
AG_{2}(3)
as a consequence of
[
41
, Theorem 1.1]
, whereas
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a
2
-
(k_{0}^{2},k_{0},\lambda)
design. Thus
\lambda>k_{0}

The group
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
contains a nontrivial

\lambda
-element
\beta
since
r_{0}=(k_{0}+1)\lambda
and
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
-acts flag-transitively on
\mathcal{D}_{0}
. Further,
A_{v_{0}}\trianglelefteq G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
is ruled out by
[
64
, Theorem 1]
. Hence
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
is either affine or an almost
simple group classified in
[
35
, Theorem 1.1 and Corollary 1.3]
since
\lambda>k_{0}
. If
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
is
an almost simple group. Then
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
is one of the groups as
in
[
35
, Table 2]
. Now,
A_{v_{0}}\nleq G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
,
v_{0}=k_{0}^{2}
and
\lambda>k_{0}
, and
r_{0}=\lambda^{2}
for
\lambda=k_{0}+1
lead to one of the following cases:

(1)

Soc(G_{\Delta}^{\Delta})\cong PSL_{w}(s)
and
k_{0}^{2}=\frac{s^{w}-1}{s-1}
;

(2)

Soc(G_{\Delta}^{\Delta})\cong PSL_{2}(s)
,
s
even, and
k_{0}^{2}=\frac{s}{2}(s\pm 1)
;

(3)

Soc(G_{\Delta}^{\Delta})\cong Sp_{w}(2)
and
k_{0}^{2}=2^{w-1}(2^{w}+1)
;

(4)

Soc(G_{\Delta}^{\Delta})\cong Sp_{w}(s)
,
s
even, and
k_{0}^{2}=\frac{s^{w}}{2}(s^{w}-1)
.

Assume that Case (1) holds. If
w\geqslant 3
, then either
(w,s,k_{0})=(4,7,20)

or
(5,3,11)
by
[
47
, A7.1(a) and A8.1]
. The former is ruled out since

PSL_{4}(7)

\trianglelefteq G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}\leqslant PGL_{4}(7)
has no
prime divisors greater than
20
, whereas
\lambda>k_{0}=20
. Thus
(w,s,k_{0})=(5,3,11)
,
PSL_{5}(3)

\trianglelefteq G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}\leqslant PGL_{5}(3)
and
\lambda=13
, and
hence
b_{0}=11\cdot 12\cdot 13
. Then
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
must have
transitive permutation representation of degree
b_{0}
since
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
acts block-transitively on
\mathcal{D}_{0}
, which is not the
case by
[
7
, Tables 8.18 and 8.19]
. Thus
w=2
and hence
k_{0}^{2}=s+1=l^{e}+1
. Then
e>1
, and hence
(k_{0},l,e)=(3,2,3)
by
[
47
, A5.1]
. Therefore,
\lambda=7
. So
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a
2
-
(9,3,7)

design and
PSL_{2}(8)

\trianglelefteq G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}\leqslant P\Gamma L_{2}(8)
. If
x
is any point of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
, then
G_{x}^{\Delta}

must have transitive permutation representation of degree
28
since
r_{0}=28
and
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
acts flag-transitively on

. However, this is impossible since
G_{x}^{\Delta}
contains a
subgroup of index at most
3
isomorphic to a Frobenius group of order
8\cdot 7
.

Assume that Case (2) holds. Then
k_{0}^{2}=2^{e-1}(2^{e}\pm 1)
since
s=2^{e}
, and hence there is a positive integer
X
such that
X^{2}=2^{e}\pm 1
with
e\geqslant 3
is odd. Then there are no
X
such that

by
[
47
, A3.1]
, whereas
X=e=3
is the unique solution
for
X^{2}=2^{e}+1

[
47
, A5.1]
. This case leads to
s=8
and
k_{0}=6
, and hence
\lambda=k_{0}+1=7
. Further,
PSL_{2}(8)

\trianglelefteq G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}\leqslant P\Gamma L_{2}(8)
. However, this case is excluded
since
r_{0}=7^{2}
does not divide the order of
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
.
Finally, Cases (3) and (4) are ruled out since
w
is even.

Assume that
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}
is of affine type either
SL_{c/h}(s^{h})\trianglelefteq G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}\leqslant\Gamma L_{w}(s)

and
k_{0}^{2}=s^{c}
, or
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}\cong A_{7}
,
k_{0}^{2}=16
,
s=2
,
h=1
,

and

by
[
35
, Table 1]
since

and

, and

for

. Also, in the two remaining cases,

. Thus,
\lambda\nmid v_{0}(v_{0}-1)
and
\lambda>k_{0}=\sqrt{v}
. Then
\mathcal{D}_{0}\cong AG_{c/h}(s^{h})
,
c/h\geqslant 3
, with all affine planes as blocks and

by Proposition
5.6
. In particular,
v_{0}=s^{c}
and
k_{0}=s^{2h}
.
Therefore,
v_{0}=k_{0}^{2}
implies
c/h=4
and so
\lambda=s^{2h}+s^{h}+1
. Thus
\lambda=k_{0}+\sqrt{k_{0}}+1>v_{1}=k_{0}+2
and hence
\lambda\nmid\left|G^{\Sigma}\right|
since
\lambda
is prime and
G^{\Sigma}
acts transitively on
\Sigma
. Therefore,
\lambda\mid\left|G_{(\Sigma)}\right|
.

If
G^{\Sigma}
is of affine type, then
v_{1}=p^{d}
with
p
prime and

. Then
s^{2h}+2=p^{d}
and hence
(s,q,n)=(5,3,3)
for

by

[
49
, Lemma 4]
. If

, then
s^{h}
is not a power of
3
, then
s^{2h}\equiv 1\pmod{3}
and hence
p=3
. So
s^{h}=1
, a contradiction.
Thus
s^{h}
is a power of
3
. Therefore, either
SL_{3}(3)\trianglelefteq G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}\leqslant GL_{3}(3)
and
s^{h}=5
or
G^{\Sigma}\cong Z_{p-1}
with

prime and
s^{h}
a power of
3
. If
G^{\Sigma}

is almost simple, since

is one of the group
A_{v_{1}}
,
PSL_{n}(q)
(

),  or
PSL_{2}(11)
or
M_{11}
with

by
[
32
, (A)]
since

with

by
[
47
, B1.1]
. Thus, one of the following holds:

(i)

G^{\Sigma}\cong AGL_{1}(p)
with
p=s^{2h}+2
prime and
s^{h}
a
power of
3
;

(ii)

ASL_{3}(3)\trianglelefteq G^{\Sigma}\leqslant AGL_{3}(3)
and
(v_{0},k_{0},\lambda,v_{1},k_{1})=(5^{4},5^{2},31,3^{3},26)
;

(iii)

Soc(G^{\Sigma})\cong A_{s^{2h}+2}
;

(iv)

Soc(G^{\Sigma})\cong PSL_{n}(q)
,
n\geqslant 2
and
(n,q)\neq(2,2),(2,3)
, and
s^{2h}+2=\frac{q^{n}-1}{q-1}
;

(v)

Soc(G^{\Sigma})\cong PSL_{2}(11)
or
M_{11}
, and and
(v_{0},k_{0},\lambda,v_{1},k_{1})=(3^{4},3^{2},13,11,10)
.

In (ii)–(v),
G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}
contains a non-solvable perfect
subgroup
N
isomorphic to
[3^{2}]:SL_{2}(3)
,
A_{s^{2h}+1}
,
[q^{n-1}]:SL_{n-1}(q)
,
A_{5}
or
PSL_{2}(11)
, respectively, acting transitively on
\Sigma\setminus\left\{\Delta\right\}

Assume that
G_{(\Sigma)}^{\Delta}\neq 1
. Then
Soc(G_{\Delta}^{\Delta})\trianglelefteq G_{(\Sigma)}^{\Delta}
since
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}

acts point-primitively on
\mathcal{D}_{0}
by the minimality of
\Sigma
. Therefore,
SL_{4}(s^{h})\trianglelefteq G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}/G_{(\Sigma)}^{\Delta}
or
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}/G_{(\Sigma)}^{\Delta}\leqslant\Gamma L_{1}(s^{h})
since
ASL_{4}(s^{h})\trianglelefteq G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}\leqslant A\Gamma L_{4}(s^{h})
. The former implies
SL_{4}(s^{h})\trianglelefteq G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}/G_{(\Delta)}^{\Sigma}
. Then
\lambda=s^{2h}+s^{h}+1
divides the order
of
G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}
, and hence the order of
G^{\Sigma}
, a
contradiction. Thus
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}/G_{(\Sigma)}^{\Delta}\leqslant\Gamma L_{1}(s^{h})
, and hence
G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}/G_{(\Delta)}^{\Sigma}\leqslant\Gamma L_{1}(s^{h})
since
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}/G_{(\Sigma)}^{\Delta}\cong G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}/G_{(\Delta)}^{\Sigma}
. Then
N\leqslant

G_{(\Delta)}^{\Sigma}
since
N

is a normal perfect group
N
. Therefore
G_{(\Delta)}^{\Sigma}
, and
hence
G_{(\Delta)}
acts transitively on
\Sigma\setminus\left\{\Delta\right\}
.

Let
B
be any block of
\mathcal{D}
such that
B\cap\Delta\neq\varnothing
. Thus
B\cap\Delta
is a block of
\mathcal{D}_{0}

preserved by
G_{(\Delta)}
Moreover,
B
is the unique block of
\mathcal{D}
intersecting
\Delta
in
B\cap\Delta
since
\mu=1
, hence
G_{(\Delta)}
preserves
B
. Then
B
intersects each element in
\Sigma\setminus\left\{\Delta\right\}
in a non-empty set since
k_{1}\geqslant 2
,
G_{(\Delta)}
preserves
B
and acts transitively on
\Sigma\setminus\left\{\Delta\right\}
. So
B
intersects each element of
B
in a
non-empty, whereas
k_{1}=v_{1}-1
.

Finally, assume that (i) holds. Hence,
G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}\cong Z_{s^{2h}+1}
with
s^{h}

a power of
3
. Then
Z_{\frac{s^{2h}+1}{u}}\leqslant\Gamma L_{1}(s^{h})
since
G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}/G_{(\Delta)}^{\Sigma}\leqslant\Gamma L_{1}(s^{h})
, and
hence
\frac{s^{2h}+1}{2u}\mid h
. Then
2u
is divisible by a primitive
prime divisor
t
of
s^{2h}+1

[
34
, Proposition 5.2.15(ii)]
. Let
\vartheta\in G_{(\Delta)}^{\Sigma}
of order
t
. Let
B
be any block of

\mathcal{D}
such that
B\cap\Delta\neq\varnothing
. Then
\left\langle\vartheta\right\rangle\leqslant G_{B\cap\Delta}
, and hence
\left\langle\vartheta\right\rangle\leqslant G_{B}
since
B
is the unique block of
\mathcal{D}
intersecting
\Delta
in
B\cap\Delta
being
\mu=1
. Then
\left\langle\vartheta\right\rangle
fixes a further element
\Delta^{\prime}\in\Sigma\setminus\left\{\Delta\right\}
such that
B\cap\Delta^{\prime}\neq\varnothing
since
\left\langle\vartheta\right\rangle\leqslant G_{B\cap\Delta}
and
k_{1}-1=s^{2h}
, and hence
\left|\left(\Delta^{\prime}\right)^{G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}}\right|\leqslant\frac{s^{2h}+1}{t}=\frac{v_{1}-1}{t}
. So,
\left(\Delta^{\prime}\right)^{G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}}\neq\Sigma\setminus\left\{\Delta\right\}
, whereas
G_{\Delta}^{\Sigma}\cong Z_{s^{2h}+1}
acts
regularly on
\Sigma\setminus\left\{\Delta\right\}
.

Assume that
G_{(\Sigma)}^{\Delta}=1
. Then
G_{(\Sigma)}\leqslant G_{(\Delta)}
, and hence
G_{(\Sigma)}\leqslant G_{(\Delta^{\prime\prime})}
for each
\Delta^{\prime\prime}\in\Sigma\setminus\left\{\Delta\right\}
since

G_{(\Sigma)}\trianglelefteq G
and
G
acts transitively on
\Sigma
.
Thus
G_{(\Sigma)}=1
, and hence
G=G^{\Sigma}
is as in one of the cases
(1)–(5). Moreover,
ASL_{4}(s^{h})\trianglelefteq G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}\leqslant A\Gamma L_{4}(s^{h})
. Then the unique admissible case is (4) with
s^{h}=q
. So
q^{2}+2=\frac{q^{n}-1}{q-1}
, and hence
q^{2}+1=q\frac{q^{n-1}-1}{q-1}
which is a contradiction.

∎

Theorem 9.2
.

Assume that Hypothesis
2.6
holds. Then one of the following holds:

(1)

\mathcal{D}
is one of the two
2
-
(16,6,2)
designs as in

, Section 1.2]
;

(2)

\mathcal{D}
is the
2
-
(45,12,3)
design as in
[

, Construction 4.2]
.

(3)

\mathcal{D}
is a
2
-
(2^{2^{j+1}}(2^{2^{j}}+2),2^{2^{j}}(2^{2^{j}}+1),2^{2^{j}}+1)
design when
2^{2^{j}}+1>3
is a Fermat prime, and the following holds:

(a)

either
\mathcal{D}_{0}\cong AG_{2}(2^{2^{j}})
and
ASL_{2}(2^{2^{j}})\unlhd G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}\leqslant A\Gamma L_{2}(2^{2^{j}})
, or
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a translation plane of order
2^{2^{j}}
and
G_{\Delta}^{\Delta}\leqslant A\Gamma L_{1}(2^{2^{j+1}})
;

(b)

\mathcal{D}_{1}
is the trivial symmetric
2
-
(2^{2^{j}}+2,2^{2^{j}}+1,2^{2^{j}})
design and either
A_{2^{2^{j}}+1}\unlhd G^{\Sigma}\leqslant A_{2^{2^{j}}+1}
or
PSL_{2}({2^{2^{j}}+1})\unlhd G^{\Sigma}\leqslant PGL_{2}({2^{2^{j}}+1})

Proof.

Recall that
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a translation plane of order
k_{0}
and

is of affine type by Proposition

. If

, then

and hence

and

.
Actually,

or

since

is permutationally isomorphic to

with both

and

subgroups
of

. Then

is one of the

2
-
(16,6,2)
designs constructed
[

, Section 1.2]
by by Corollary

since

.

, then

and

by

, Main Theorem and Examples 1.1 and 1.2]
.

Assume that

. Then, it is easy to verify

when

. Then

is cyclic of order
\lambda
and acts point-regularly on

since

and the order of

. However, this is impossible since the order of

, where

\mathcal{D}
, is divisible by

, because

acts flag-transitively on

\mathcal{D}
. Thus

, and hence either

, or

since

. If

, and hence the assertions (1) and (2) follow from

.

. Then

, or

, which is not a power of a prime. Thus

, and hence either

, (A) and (B)]
since

with

a Fermat prime. The latter implies

and hence

and

. Therefore,

, or

, which is (3b). This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

Let
\mathcal{D}=(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{B})
be any
2
-
(v,k,\lambda)
design with
\lambda
a prime number, and
G
be a
flag-transitive, point-imprimitive automorphism group of
\mathcal{D}
preserving a nontrivial
partition
\Sigma
of
\mathcal{P}
with
v_{1}
classes of size
v_{0}
. Possibly by substituting
\Sigma
with a
G
-invariant partition of
\mathcal{P}
finer that
\Sigma
, we may assume that
\Sigma
is minimal with respect to the ordering
\preceq
defined in the Subsection
2.1
. Then
\lambda\mid r
by Theorem
2.2
. Further, either
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is either symmetric
1
-design with
k_{1}=v_{1}-1
, or
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is a (possibly trivial)
2
-design by Theorem
2.4
(2). Actually, the latter cannot occur by Theorem
8.1
. Thus
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is symmetric
1
-design with
k_{1}=v_{1}-1
, and hence the assertion now follows from Theorem
9.2
. This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Corollary
1.2
.

Let
\mathcal{D}=(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{B})
is a
2
-
(v,k,\lambda)
design with
\lambda
a prime number admitting a
flag-transitive automorphism group
G
, not isomorphic to the three possibilities of Theorem
1.1
. Then
G^{\Sigma}
acts
G
acts point-primitively on
\mathcal{D}
by Theorem Theorem
1.1
. Moreover, either
(r,\lambda)=1
or
\lambda\mid r
since
\lambda
is a prime number. Therefore, either
G
is of affine type, or
G
is an almost simple group by
[
62
, Theorem]
or
[
63
, Theorem 1]
, respectively.
∎
